We prove various properties and comparison theorems satisfied by the above cohomologies with compact support. Most of these results are the compactly supported analogs of the ones we have in the usual case (see
[
15
]
,
[
16
]
).

3.1.
De Rham cohomology with compact support

We begin by reviewing the definition of compactly supported de Rham cohomology of rigid analytic varieties introduced by Chiarellotto
[
8
]
.

3.1.1.
Definition of de Rham cohomology with compact support

Let
L=K,C
and let
X
be a smooth rigid analytic variety over
L
.
Recall that the de Rham cohomology of
X\in{\rm Sm}_{L}
is defined as

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X):={\rm R}\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X}^{\bullet})\in{\mathscr{F}}{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box}),

where
\Omega_{X}^{\bullet}
is the complex of sheaves of differential forms and
{\mathscr{F}}{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})
is filtered
\infty
-category of
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})
.
As in Section
2.1
, we set in
{\mathscr{F}}{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X):=\operatorname{colim}_{Z\in\Phi}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X\setminus Z),\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)

\displaystyle:=[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X)],

(3.1)

and write
H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X):=H^{i}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)
for the associated cohomology groups. We have

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}),

where the compactly supported coherent cohomology is defined by an analog of formula (
3.1
).
We have equipped
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)
with the Hodge filtration

F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X):={\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{\geq r}).

Hence
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)
is a (solid) filtered dg
L
-algebra. We refer the reader to Section
2.1
for its basic properties.

3.1.2.
Definition of
{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology with compact support

The construction is analogous to the one above, using the definition of the rigid analytic
{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology from
[
16
, Section 3.3]
.

(i)
Rigid analytic
{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology
. Let us first recall how the usual
{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}
-cohomology is defined. Denote by
{\mathscr{M}}_{C}^{\rm ss}
the category of semistable formal models and let
{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{C}^{\rm ss}
. By regarding the crystalline structure sheaf
{\mathscr{O}}_{\operatorname{cr}}
and the divided power sheaves
{\mathscr{J}}^{[r]}
,
r\geq 0
, as sheaves with values in condensed abelian groups, we can define the absolute crystalline cohomology
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
,
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{J}}^{[r]})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})
. By completing with respect to its Hodge filtration and then taking the
\eta
-sheafification, we define sheaves
F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}
,
r\geq 0
on
{\rm Sm}_{C,\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}
with values in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}^{+})
. The
{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology is then given by:

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}})\in{\mathscr{D}}({\rm Mod}_{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}}^{\rm solid})

\displaystyle F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,F^{r}{{\mathscr{A}}}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}})\in{\mathscr{D}}({\rm Mod}_{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}}^{\rm solid}),\quad\text{for }r\geq 0.

It is a (solid) dg filtered
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-algebra equipped with a continuous action of
{\mathscr{G}}_{K}
if
X
is defined over
K
.

We have a canonical map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\Box})

\displaystyle\vartheta:\quad

\displaystyle F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),\quad r\geq 0.

(ii)
Compactly supported
{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology
. Let
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
.
Proceeding as in Section
2.1
, we obtain compactly supported rigid analytic

{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}^{+})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),\quad F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),\quad r\geq 0,

starting with the rigid analytic
{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology from the previous paragraph. It is a solid dg filtered
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-algebra equipped with a continuous action of

{\mathscr{G}}_{K}
if
X
is defined over
K
. It satisfies the properties listed in Section
2.1
.
We have a canonical map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\Box})

\displaystyle\vartheta_{c}:\quad

\displaystyle F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X),\quad r\geq 0.

3.1.3.
Comparison results

In this section, we give the analogs for compactly supported
{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}
-cohomology of the comparison results that we have in the classical case. We first recall the results from
[
16
, Section 3.3.2]
formulating them in the solid world.

(i)
Product formula
.
Let
X
be a smooth rigid analytic variety over
K
. We define filtrations on
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
and
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
by the formulas

\displaystyle F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle:=\operatorname{colim}_{i+j\geq r}F^{i}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}F^{j}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+},

\displaystyle F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle:=\operatorname{colim}_{i+j\geq r}F^{i}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}F^{j}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}.

Remark 3.2
.

For
r\geq 0
, we clearly have a distinguished triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}})
:

F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

Moreover, since
\operatorname{colim}_{Z}(-)
commutes with
(-)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}
, we have a canonical quasi-isomorphism

F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\operatorname{colim}_{Z}F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X\setminus Z)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

Recall that in the classical case we have:

Lemma 3.3
.

(
[
16
, Lemma 3.17]
)

Let
X
be a smooth rigid analytic variety over
K
. Then, there is
a filtered quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}^{+})
:

\iota_{\rm BK}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

Proof.

We can work étale locally. Consider the maps:

\displaystyle\iota_{{\rm BK},r}:({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{L}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+})/F^{r}

\displaystyle\to({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}/{\mathscr{O}}_{L}^{\times})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{L}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{O}}_{\overline{K}}^{\times}/{\mathscr{O}}_{L}^{\times})_{{\bf Q}_{p}})/F^{r}

(3.4)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\cup}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/{\mathscr{O}}_{L}^{\times})_{{\bf Q}_{p}})/F^{r}\leftarrow({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}})/F^{r}

for
{\mathscr{X}}
the base change to
{\mathscr{O}}_{C}
of a semistable model
{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}
over a finite extension
L
of
K
, and where we have defined

F^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{L}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}):=({\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{L}F^{r}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}\to\Omega^{1}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{L}F^{r-1}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}\to\Omega^{2}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{L}F^{r-2}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}\to\cdots).

The first arrow in (
3.4
) is a quasi-isomorphism (use the fact that the ring
{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}
is the Hodge completed crystalline cohomology of the base ring
{\mathscr{O}}_{\overline{K}}
). For the second map, we know that, by the Künneth formula, we have quasi-isomorphisms:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L},n}/{\mathscr{O}}_{L,n}^{\times})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{O}}_{\overline{K},n}^{\times}/{\mathscr{O}}_{L,n}^{\times})\xrightarrow[\sim]{\cup}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{n}/{\mathscr{O}}_{L,n}^{\times}),\quad\text{ for }n\geq 1.

By taking the derived inverse limit over
n\geq 1
, we get a quasi-isomorphisms:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}/{\mathscr{O}}_{L}^{\times})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{L}}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{O}}_{\overline{K}}^{\times}/{\mathscr{O}}_{L}^{\times})\xrightarrow[\sim]{\cup}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/{\mathscr{O}}_{L}^{\times}).

We then use
[
7
, Lemma A.9]
to identify the left hand side with the solid tensor product:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}/{\mathscr{O}}_{L}^{\times})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{L}}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{O}}_{\overline{K}}^{\times}/{\mathscr{O}}_{L}^{\times})={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}/{\mathscr{O}}_{L}^{\times})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{O}}_{\overline{K}}^{\times}/{\mathscr{O}}_{L}^{\times}).

Inverting
p
, we obtain that the second map in (
3.4
) is a quasi-isomorphism.
The third arrow in (
3.4
) is given by the base change from
{\mathscr{O}}_{F}^{\times}
to
{\mathscr{O}}_{L}^{\times}
and it is a quasi-isomorphism by
[
30
, Corollary 2.4]
.
By taking the derived limit
{\mathrm{R}}\lim_{r}\iota_{{\rm BK},r}
, we get a quasi-isomorphism:

\hat{\iota}_{\rm BK}:{\mathrm{R}}\lim_{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{L}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+})/F^{r}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}.

To conclude it suffices to show that we have a quasi-isomorphism:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{L}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathrm{R}}\lim_{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{L}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+})/F^{r}.

But this can be done as in
[
16
, Lemma 3.17]
, by writing the explicit definition of the filtration and arguing degreewise.
∎

Applying the previous lemma to the varieties
X
and
X\setminus Z
, for
Z\in\Phi_{X}
, and using that the tensor product commutes with colimits, we obtain a filtered quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})
for the compactly supported cohomologies:

\iota_{{\rm BK},c}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

(3.5)

Note that the compatibility with the filtrations follows from the Remark
3.2
.

(ii)
Projection from
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology to de Rham cohomology
. We can now compare the
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
- and de Rham cohomologies.

Recall that we have:

Proposition 3.6
.

(
[
15
, Prop. 3.11]
)

Let
X
be a smooth rigid analytic variety over
C
. Then,

(1)

We have a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\Box})

\vartheta:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X).

(3.7)

(2)

More generally, for
r\geq 0
, we have a natural distinguished triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}^{+}})

F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{t}}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\vartheta}}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)

(3.8)

(3)

For
r\geq 0
, we have a natural distinguished triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}^{+}})

F^{r+1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta_{X}}}{{\to}}\bigoplus_{i\leq r}{\rm R}\Gamma(X,\Omega^{i}_{X})(r-i)[-i]

(3.9)

Proof.

The proof from
[
15
, Prop. 3.11]
still works in the solid setting: working étale locally, we use the
\iota_{\rm BK}
from Lemma
3.3
to reduce to similar statement for the de Rham cohomology instead of the crystalline one.
∎

We deduce from the previous proposition its analog in the compactly supported case:

Proposition 3.10
.

Let
X
be a smooth rigid analytic variety over
C
. Then,

(1)

We have a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\Box})

\vartheta_{c}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X).

(2)

More generally, for
r\geq 0
, we have a natural distinguished triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}^{+}})

F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{t}}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\vartheta_{c}}}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)

(3.11)

(3)

For
r\geq 0
, we have a natural distinguished triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}^{+}})

F^{r+1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta_{X}}}{{\to}}\bigoplus_{i\leq r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{i}_{X})(r-i)[-i]

(3.12)

Proof.

The proposition follows from Proposition
3.7
, applying it to the smooth varieties
X
and
X\setminus Z
, for
Z
quasi-compact. As usual, we use that the solid tensor product commutes with colimits for the first claim. The second and third points come from the fact that, by definition, we have distinguished triangles in
{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}^{+}})
and in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\Box})
, respectively:

\displaystyle F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+})

\displaystyle\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+})\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}),

\displaystyle F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)

\displaystyle\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X),\quad\text{ for }r\geq 0.

∎

3.1.4.
Computations

In this section, we will give an explicit formula to compute the quotients of the compactly supported
{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}
-cohomology of
X
when
X
is a Stein variety (and in particular, when
X
is the affine space). These quotients will intervene in the computation of the compactly supported pro-étale cohomology of
X
, and the following results will allow us to define a trace map for the compactly supported pro-étale cohomology in Section
7.3
.

(i)
Stein varieties.
Let
X
be a smooth Stein variety of dimension
d
over
K
.
We will see later that in that case
H^{i}_{c}(X,\Omega^{j}_{X})=0
for
i\neq d
and all
j
in
\{0,\cdots,d\}
(see Proposition
5.10
). Thus, we obtain that the de Rham cohomology
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)
is computed by the complex:

\displaystyle H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{\bullet}):=(H^{d}_{c}(X,\mathscr{O}_{X})\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})\to\dots\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d}))[-d],

(3.13)

\displaystyle F^{i}H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{\bullet}):=H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{\geq i})=(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{i})\to\dots\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d}))[-d-i].

It follows that in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})
:

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\simeq(H^{d}_{c}(X,\mathscr{O}_{X})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\dots\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})[-d].

Concerning the filtration, we have in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})

\displaystyle F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{i+j\geq r}F^{i}H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{\bullet})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}F^{j}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}

\displaystyle\simeq(H^{d}_{c}(X,\mathscr{O}_{X})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}F^{r}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}F^{r-1}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\cdots)[-d].

This yields in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})

\displaystyle\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X,r)

\displaystyle:=({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}

\displaystyle\simeq(H^{d}_{c}(X,\mathscr{O}_{X})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r})\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r-1})

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\to\dots\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r-d}))[-d].

Lemma 3.14
.

(1)

We have an exact sequence in
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}
:

\begin{split}0\to(H^{d}_{c}(X_{C},\Omega^{i-d})/{\rm Im}\,d)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}{\rm gr}^{r-i+d-1}_{F}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to H^{i}\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X,r)\to H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r-i+d-1})\to 0.\end{split}

(2)

In degree
2d
, we have an isomorphism in
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}
:

H^{2d}\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X,r)\simeq H_{\operatorname{dR},c}^{2d}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r-d}).

Proof.

This follows from the fact that,
for
0\leq j\leq d
, the differential
\tilde{d}_{j+d}
of the complex
\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X,r)

factorizes as :

H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{j})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r-j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm Id}\otimes{\rm Id}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{j})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r-j-1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d_{j}\otimes{\rm Id}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{j+1})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r-j-1})

where the first arrow is surjective with kernel
H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{j})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}(F^{r-j-1}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r-j})
.
∎

(ii)
Affine space.

For example, let
X={\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{d}
be the rigid analytic affine space over
K
of dimension
d\geq 1
. We will see in Section
5
that we have:

H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\simeq\begin{cases}0\text{ for }i\neq 2d,\\
K\text{ for }i=2d.\end{cases}

(3.15)

Lemma 3.16
.

Let
r^{\prime}:=r-i+d-1
.
We have isomorphisms in
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}
:

H^{i}\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X,r)\simeq\begin{cases}(H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{i-d})/{\rm Im}\,d)\otimes^{\Box}_{F}{\rm gr}^{r^{\prime}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\simeq(H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{i-d})/{\rm Ker}\,d)\otimes^{\Box}_{F}{\rm gr}^{r^{\prime}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}&\mbox{ if }d\leq i\leq 2d-1,\\
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r-d}&\mbox{ if }i=2d,\\
0&\mbox{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proof.

It suffices to use Lemma
3.14
and the isomorphisms (
3.15
).
∎

3.2.
Rigid analytic Hyodo-Kato cohomology with compact support

We now define the Hyodo-Kato cohomology with compact support for smooth rigid analytic varieties over
K,C
. The construction is as in Section
2.1
, using the definitions of
[
15
]
,
[
16
]
of the rigid analytic Hyodo-Kato cohomologies. We then prove, using the classical case, that we have a compactly supported version of the Hyodo-Kato (iso)morphisms.

3.2.1.
Definition

We will give three definitions of Hyodo-Kato cohomologies: the arithmetic one (when the variety is defined over
K
) and the geometric and completed ones (when the variety is defined over
C
).

(i)
Rigid analytic Hyodo-Kato cohomology.

Let
L=K,C
and
X\in{\rm Sm}_{L}
. As in section
3.1.2
(i.e., by regarding the crystalline sheaves as sheaves with values in the solid world), we can define the solid versions of the Hyodo-Kato cohomologies from
[
15
, 4.2, 4.3]
:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(F_{\Box}),\text{ if }L=K,\quad\text{ and }\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(F^{\rm nr}_{\Box}),\text{ if }L=C,

and, if
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
, the completed Hyodo-Kato cohomology (see
[
16
, Sec. 4.1.1]
):

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(\breve{F}_{\Box}).

Here
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(-)
denotes the derived
\infty
-category of solid
(\varphi,N)
-modules; similarly we define the category
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N,{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(-)
.

Lemma 3.17
.

For any
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
, we have a canonical quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(\breve{F}_{\Box})
:

\beta:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\breve{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X).

Proof.

We can work étale locally. Let
{\mathscr{X}}
be the base change of a formal model
{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}
defined over
L
a finite extension of
K
. We have a quasi-isomorphism:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{0}/{\mathscr{O}}_{F_{L},n}^{0})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{F_{L},n}}{\mathscr{O}}_{\breve{F},n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{0}/{\mathscr{O}}_{\breve{F},n}),\quad\text{ for }n\geq 1

where
{\mathscr{O}}_{F_{L}}:=W(k_{L})
. By taking the derived inverse limit over
n
and inverting
p
, we get a quasi-ismorphism:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}_{0})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{L}}}_{{F_{L}}}{\breve{F}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}({\mathscr{X}}_{0}),

where
\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{L}}}
is the derived completion. Since
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}_{0})
is built from Banach spaces, we can pass to the solid tensor product.
∎

(ii)
Compactly supported Hyodo-Kato cohomology.

Proceeding as in Section
2.1
, we obtain compactly supported Hyodo-Kato cohomology
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)
from
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)
. This is a solid dg
F
-algebra if
L=K
, and a dg
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
-algebra if
L=C
, equipped with a Frobenius
\varphi
, a monodromy operator
N
and a continuous action of
{\mathscr{G}}_{K}
if
X
is defined over
K
. It satisfies all of the properties in Section
2.1
.

If
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
, we define as above its completed version
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}(X)
, using
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)
. It is a solid dg
\breve{F}
-algebra equipped with a Frobenius
\varphi
, a monodromy operator
N
and a continuous action of
{\mathscr{G}}_{K}
if
X
is defined over
K
. It satisfies all of the properties in Section
2.1
. Moreover, since the tensor product commutes with colimits, the quasi-isomorphism from Lemma
3.17
applied to
X,X\setminus Z
, for
Z
quasi-compact, induces a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(\breve{F}_{\Box})
:

\beta_{c}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\breve{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}(X).

3.2.2.
Hyodo-Kato morphisms

We will define two types of Hyodo-Kato morphisms: Hyodo-Kato-to-de Rham and Hyodo-Kato-to-
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
.
Recall the result for usual cohomology:

Proposition 3.18
.

(
[
16
, Theorem 4.6]
)

For
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
, we have natural quasi-isomorphisms
in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\Box})
and
{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}})
, respectively,

\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),\quad\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),

where
F_{L}=F^{\operatorname{nr}},\breve{F}
and
*=-,\breve{F}
, that are compatible with the maps
\theta:{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}\to C
and
\vartheta
from (
3.7
).

Proof.

We can work étale locally. The local quasi-isomorphisms are constructed in
[
16
]
and the construction goes through in the condensed setting. Recall, that the main point is to show that we have a quasi-isomorphism:

\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{st}}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}_{1}^{0})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}.

∎

We deduce:

Proposition 3.19
.

Let
X
be a smooth rigid analytic variety over
L=K,C
.

(1)

There is a Hyodo-Kato morphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(F_{L,\Box})

\iota_{\operatorname{HK},c}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X){\to}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)

(where
*=-,\breve{F}
) that is compatible with its usual version
\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X){\to}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X).

(2)

If
L=C
, it induces quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\Box})
and in
{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}}
, respectively:

\displaystyle\iota_{\operatorname{HK},c}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X),

\displaystyle\iota_{\operatorname{HK},c}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),

where
F_{L}=F^{\operatorname{nr}},\breve{F}
and
*=-,\breve{F}
, that are compatible with the maps
\theta:{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}\to C
and
\vartheta_{c}
from (
3.10
).

Proof.

The proposition follows immediately from Proposition
3.18
applied to
Y=X,X\setminus Z
, for
Z
quasi-compact.
∎

4.
Overconvergent de Rham and Hyodo-Kato cohomologies with compact support

In this section, we define compactly supported de Rham,
{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}
- and Hyodo-Kato cohomologies for smooth dagger varieties over
L=K,C
. To do that we first define the compactly supported cohomology of a dagger affinoid
X
as the colimit of the cohomologies of the rigid affinoids
X_{h}
forming a presentation of the dagger structure, but supported in the rigid completion
\widehat{X}
of
X
. We then globalize this definition.

We prove various properties satisfied by these cohomologies, which are, as before, compactly supported versions of the results from
[
15
]
,
[
16
]
. In particular, we compare them with their rigid analytic analogs defined in the previous section.

4.1.
Definition of de Rham cohomology with compact support

We first introduce the definition of overconvergent de Rham cohomology with compact support using presentations of dagger structures (see
[
38
, Appendix]
,
[
15
, Section 3.2.1]
for the definition and properties of presentations of dagger structures).

(i)
Local definition
. Let
X
be a dagger affinoid over
L=K,C
. Let
{\rm pres}(X)=\{X_{h}\}
and let
\widehat{X}
be the rigid completion of
X
.
Define in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})
:

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X)

\displaystyle:={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial\{X\}_{h}):=\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X}),

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)

\displaystyle:={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(\{X\}_{h}):=[{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X)]

where
{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X):=\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})
is the overconvergent de Rham cohomology from
[
16
]
. We have a natural filtration on
{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)
:

F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X):=[F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\to\operatorname{colim}_{h}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X})],\quad r\geq 0.

We can write

F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X):=\operatorname{colim}_{h}[F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X})]=:\operatorname{colim}_{h}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},\widehat{X}}(X_{h}).

(4.1)

As in the pro-étale case, this makes it clear that the functor
F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)
is covariant for open embeddings.

(ii)
Globalization
. For a general smooth dagger variety
X
over
L
, using the natural equivalence

{\rm CSh}({\rm SmAff}^{\dagger}_{L,{\rm an}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm CSh}({\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{L,{\rm an}})\

(4.2)

we define the cosheaves
8
8

8

See
[
28
, Definition 5.5.4.1]
for a definition of a cosheaf in a
\infty
-category.

{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}
and
F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}
,
r\geq 0
, on
X_{\operatorname{an}}
with values in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})
as the cosheaves associated to the precosheaves defined by
U\mapsto{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(U)
and
U\mapsto F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(U)
,
r\geq 0
, for
U\in{\rm SmAff}^{\dagger}_{L}
,
U\subset X
an admissible open. We set

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X):={\rm R}\Gamma(X,{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{dR},c})\quad\text{ and }F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X):={\rm R}\Gamma(X,F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}),\quad r\geq 0.

We obtain a solid filtered dg
L
-algebra.

Lemma 4.3
.

(1)

(Local-global compatibility)

If
X
is a smooth dagger affinoid over
L=K,C
, there is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{F}}{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})
:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X).

(2)

(Passing to completion)
If
X
is smooth and partially proper over
L=K,C
then there is a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{F}}{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}({X})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(\widehat{X}).

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is analogous to the one of Lemma
2.35
.
∎

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger Stein variety over
L=K,C
and let
\{U_{n}\}_{n}
be an admissible strict covering of
X
with dagger affinoids
U_{n}\Subset U_{n+1}
. Then there are natural quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})
:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{colim}_{n}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},U_{n}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{colim}_{n}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},U_{n}}(U_{n+1}).

Remark 4.5
.

In particular, if we define the cohomology of the boundary of
X
from Proposition
4.4
as

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X):=\operatorname{colim}_{n}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X\setminus U_{n}),

we have a distinguished triangle:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X).

(4.6)

As in the rigid analytic set-up.

Proof.

Let
X
and
\{U_{n}\}
be as in the statement of the proposition. For each
n
, let
\{U_{n,h}\}_{h}
be a presentation of
U_{n}
. Then,

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{colim}_{n}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(U_{n})\xleftarrow{\sim}\operatorname{colim}_{n}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(U_{n})\xleftarrow{\sim}\operatorname{colim}_{n}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(U_{n})

where the first quasi-isomorphism comes from the fact that
{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}
is a cosheaf, the second one is the local-global compatibility from Lemma
4.3
, and the third one is the de Rham analog of the one from Proposition
2.37
.
For
h\in{\bf N}
, from the excision (
2.1
), we have

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}^{\rm GK}(U_{n})={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},\widehat{U}_{n}}(U_{n,h})\xleftarrow{\sim}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},\widehat{U}_{n}}(\widehat{X}).

We conclude the proof using the quasi-isomorphisms
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X})\xleftarrow{\sim}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)
and
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}\setminus\widehat{U}_{n})\xleftarrow{\sim}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X\setminus U_{n})
.
∎

4.2.
Overconvergent
{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology with compact support

Proceeding as for the de Rham cohomology, we define compactly supported
{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}
-cohomology via presentation of dagger structures.

4.2.1.
Definition

Let
X
be a smooth dagger affinoid over
C
. Taking a presentation
{\rm pres}(X)=\{X_{h}\}
of
X
as above we define overconvergent
{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology with compact support

F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\in{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}^{+}),\quad\mbox{for }r\geq 0,

using the overconvergent
{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology

F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}):=\operatorname{colim}_{h}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+})

from
[
15
, Sec. 3.4.1]
. The functor
F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
is covariant for open embeddings.

The
\eta
-analytic cosheafification of
F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
gives us a cosheaf
F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{\operatorname{cr},c}
on any smooth dagger variety
X
over
C
, with values in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}^{+})
. The filtered
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology
with compact support is then defined as

F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):={\rm R}\Gamma(X,F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{\operatorname{cr},c})\in{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}^{+}),\quad r\geq 0.

Lemma 4.7
.

(1)

(Local-global compatibility)

If
X
is a smooth dagger affinoid over
C
, there is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{F}}{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}^{+}})
:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

(2)

(Passing to completion)
If
X
is smooth and partially proper over
C
then there is a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{F}}{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}^{+}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}({X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(\widehat{X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is analogous to the one of Lemma
2.35
.
∎

We have an analog of Proposition
4.4
in this setting (using
[
16
, Cor. 4.32]
) hence we see that if
X
is Stein and smooth, we have a distinguished triangle

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+})

(4.8)

where
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}):=\operatorname{colim}_{n}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}((X\setminus U_{n})/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+})
, for
\{U_{n}\}
a Stein covering of
X
.

4.2.2.
Properties

We will now prove properties of overconvergent
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology that do not require Hyodo-Kato cohomology.
We have canonical maps, in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})
and
{\mathscr{F}}{\mathscr{D}}(K_{\Box})
, respectively:

\displaystyle\vartheta_{c}:\quad

\displaystyle F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X),\quad X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C},\quad r\geq 0,

\displaystyle\iota_{{\rm BK},c}:\quad

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),\quad X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{K},

induced by their rigid analytic analogs.

Proposition 4.9
.

(1)

(Projection)
Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
. Then,

(a)

The map
\vartheta_{c}
yields a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\Box})
:

\vartheta_{c}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X).

(b)

More generally, for
r\geq 0
, we have a natural distinguished triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}^{+}})

F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},c})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{t}}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\vartheta_{c}}}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)

(4.10)

(c)

(Devissage)
For
r\geq 0
, we have a natural distinguished triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}^{+}})

F^{r+1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to\bigoplus_{i\leq r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{i}_{X})(r-i)[-i]

(4.11)

(2)

(Product formula)
Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
K
. The map
\iota_{{\rm BK},c}

defined above yields a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{F}}{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})
:

\iota_{{\rm BK},c}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

Remark 4.12
.

In Proposition
4.9
(2),
the filtration on
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
is defined by the formula

F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):=\operatorname{colim}_{i+j\geq r}F^{i}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}F^{j}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+},

and, if
X
is a dagger affinoid, we also define

F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):=\operatorname{colim}_{i+j\geq r}F^{i}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}F^{j}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}.

Note that we have a local-global compatibility between the two, using Lemma
4.3
.
By the same argument as in Remark
3.2
, we see that we have a distinguished triangle

F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

We also have
F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):=\operatorname{colim}_{h}F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{U})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
, for
\{X_{h}\}
a dagger presentation of
X
.

Proof.

To prove the point (1)(a) of the proposition, it suffices to argue locally, then apply the analogous quasi-ismorphism obtained in the rigid analytic case (
3.7
) to the varieties
X_{h}
and
X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X}
and we conclude using that the solid tensor product commutes with colimits.

The arguments are similar for the distinguished triangles (1)(b) (using (
3.8
)) and (1)(c) (using (
3.9
)), and for the quasi-isomorphism (2) (using (
3.3
) and the previous remark for the compatibility with the filtrations).
∎

4.3.
Overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomology with compact support

We now define overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomologies with compact support via similar methods. As before, we have three definitions (the arithmetic Hyodo-Kato cohomology, if
X\in{\rm Sm}_{K}^{\dagger}
, and the geometric and completed ones, if
X
is defined over
C
). We will obtain Hyodo-Kato (iso)morphisms, compatible with the maps from the above proposition.

4.3.1.
Definition

Let
L=K,C
and
X
be a smooth dagger affinoid variety over
L
. Starting from the overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomologies

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X):=\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X_{h})

from
[
16
, §4.2.1]
we obtain, as in the de Rham case, the local compactly supported Hyodo-Kato cohomologies:

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(F_{\Box}),\text{ if }L=K,\quad\text{ and }\quad{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(F^{\rm nr}_{\Box}),\text{ if }L=C,

and, if
X
is over
C
, the completed Hyodo-Kato cohomology:

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}(X)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}({\breve{F}}_{\Box}).

These functors are covariant for open embeddings.

This allows us to define cosheaves
{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}
and
{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}
on any smooth dagger variety
X
over
L
. We define

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X):={\rm R}\Gamma(X,{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c})\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}({F_{L}}_{\Box}),\text{ with }F_{L}=F,F^{\rm nr},\breve{F}\text{ respectively, and }*=-,\breve{F}.

It is a solid dg
F_{L}
-algebra equipped with a Frobenius, monodromy action, and a continuous action of
{\mathscr{G}}_{K}
if
X
is defined over
K
. If
X
is Stein, we have a distinguished triangle

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(\partial X)

(4.13)

where
*=-,\breve{F}
and
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(\partial X):=\operatorname{colim}_{n}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X\setminus U_{n})
, for a Stein covering
\{U_{n}\}
.

Lemma 4.14
.

For any
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
, we have a canonical quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}({\breve{F}}_{\Box})
:

\beta_{c}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\breve{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}(X).

Proof.

We can work locally. Let
X
be a smooth dagger affinoid over
C
. Then,

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}\breve{F}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{colim}_{h}[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X})]\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}\breve{F}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{colim}_{h}[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}\breve{F}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}\breve{F}]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\beta]{\sim}\operatorname{colim}_{h}[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X_{h})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X})]={\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}(X)

where
F_{L}=W(k_{L})
. The first quasi-isomorphism is obtained by commutativity of the solid tensor product with colimits and the second one is the map
\beta
from Lemma
3.17
.
∎

4.3.2.
Overconvergent Hyodo-Kato morphisms

Let
X
be a smooth dagger affinoid over
K
or
C
. The rigid analytic Hyodo-Kato morphism from Section
3.2.2
induces a morphism:

\displaystyle\iota^{\natural}_{{\rm HK},c}:{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)

coming from the commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X_{h})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X}),}

where
\{X_{h}\}
is the presentation of
X
,
\widehat{X}
– the rigid analytic completion of
X
, and
*=-,\breve{F}
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
K
or
C
. The previous morphism
\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}^{\natural}
is clearly functorial for open immersions hence globalizes to give a morphism

\displaystyle\iota_{{\rm HK},c}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)

compatible with its classical version
\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X){\to}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X).

Proposition 4.15
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
.

The Hyodo-Kato morphism
\iota_{\operatorname{HK},c}
induces quasi-isomorphisms (where
F_{L}=F^{\operatorname{nr}},\breve{F}
and
*=-,\breve{F}
) in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\Box})
and
{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}})
, respectively:

\iota_{\operatorname{HK},c}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X),\quad\iota_{\operatorname{HK},c}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

that are compatible with the maps
\theta:{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}\to C
and
\vartheta_{c}
from Section
4.2.2
.

Proof.

It suffices to argue locally. Let
X
be a smooth dagger affinoid over
L
. Then,

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}C

\displaystyle=\operatorname{colim}_{h}[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X_{h})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X})]\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}C

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{colim}_{h}[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X_{h})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}C\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}C]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}]{\sim}\operatorname{colim}_{h}[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X})]={\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X).

The first quasi-isomorphism is obtained by commutativity of the solid tensor product with colimits and the second one is the Hyodo-Kato morphism from Proposition
3.18
.
∎

As an immediate application of the overconvergent Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphisms we get the local-global compatibility for (geometric and completed) Hyodo-Kato and
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology with compact support:

Corollary 4.16
.

(Local-global compatibility)
Let
X
be a smooth dagger affinoid over
C
.

(1)

There is a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(F_{L,\Box})
:

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)

where
*=-,\breve{F}
.

(2)

The canonical morphism in
{\mathscr{F}}{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}})

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

We start with the proof for the Hyodo-Kato cohomology. The proof is the same as
[
16
, Prop. 3.5]
. It suffices to show that for any analytic dagger affinoid hypercovering
U_{\bullet}
of
X
, the map :

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)\leftarrow{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(U_{\bullet})

(4.17)

is a quasi-isomorphism.
Consider the following commutative diagram (where
F_{L}=F^{\rm nr},\breve{F}
and
*=-,\breve{F}
) in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\Box})
:

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\rm HK}^{\natural}}

\sim

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(U_{\bullet})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\rm HK}^{\natural}}

\sim

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(U_{\bullet})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}

The vertical arrows are quasi-isomorphisms by (the proof of) Proposition
4.15
and the bottom horizontal one by (the proof of) Lemma
4.3
. It follows that the upper horizontal arrow is a quasi-isomorphism as well.

It remains to check that this implies the quasi-isomorphism (
4.17
). For that, it is enough to show that if
K^{\bullet}
is in
{\mathscr{D}}({F_{L,\Box}})
such that
K^{\bullet}\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}C
is acyclic then
K^{\bullet}
itself is acyclic. To see that, writing
C\simeq F_{L}\oplus W
with
W
a
F_{L}
-Banach space, we obtain

0=H^{*}(K^{\bullet}\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}C)=H^{*}(K^{\bullet})\oplus H^{*}(K^{\bullet}\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F_{L}}W)

so
H^{*}(K^{\bullet})=0
, and this concludes the proof of the first point.

The second point follows from the first one and from Lemma
4.3
by the exact same argument as in
[
16
, Corollary 4.28]
.
∎

4.4.
Comparison with the rigid analytic constructions

We can now compare the overconvergent compactly supported cohomologies with the rigid analytic ones.
Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
L=K,C
and let
\widehat{X}
be its completion. Then, we have:

Proposition 4.18
.

Assume that
X
is partially proper. Then,

(1)

There is a natural quasi-isomorphim in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(*_{\Box})
, for
*=F,F^{\operatorname{nr}}
, respectively:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},c}({X})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},c}(\widehat{X}).

(4.19)

(2)

Let
L=C
. There are compatible natural quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(\breve{F}_{\Box})
and
{\mathscr{F}}{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}})
, respectively:

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}({X})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}(\widehat{X}),\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}({X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(\widehat{X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

They are compatible with the map (
4.19
).

(3)

The morphisms in (1) and (2) are compatible with the Hyodo-Kato morphisms, i.e., we have the commutative diagrams (where
*=-,\breve{F}
) in
{\mathscr{D}}(F_{L,\Box})
and
{\mathscr{D}}(\breve{F}_{\Box})
, respectively:

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}({X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK},c}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(\widehat{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK},c}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}({X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(\widehat{X}),}

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}({X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK},c}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}(\widehat{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK},c}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}({X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(\widehat{X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).}

Proof.

These results follow from the de Rham and
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology cases (see Lemma
4.3
and Lemma
4.7
) using the Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphisms from Proposition
3.18
and Proposition
4.15
.
∎

5.
Dualities for de Rham and Hyodo-Kato cohomologies

We prove Poincaré duality results for the de Rham and Hyodo-Kato cohomologies of partially proper smooth rigid analytic varieties over
L=K,C
. The de Rham duality will follow from the coherent duality theorem for smooth Stein varieties first proved by Chiarellotto in
[
8
]
. We then obtain the same results for quasi-compact smooth dagger varieties over
K
or
C
. In the dagger affinoid case, the coherent and de Rham dualities were shown by Grosse-Klönne in
[
21
, Th. 4.4, Th. 4.9]
.

The trace maps constructed below will allow us to define a trace map for
p
-adic pro-étale cohomology in Section
7.3
. This trace morphism will then be used in
[
12
]
to prove Poincaré duality for
p
-adic pro-étale cohomology of rigid analytic varieties.

5.1.
Coherent duality: classical approach

We will briefly review the classical coherent dualities for rigid analytic varieties.

5.1.1.
Rigid analytic setting

Let
L=K,C
and let
X
be a rigid analytic variety over
L
. In this section we will discuss the following well-known result.

Theorem 5.1
.

(
Serre duality
)

Let
X
be a Stein or a proper rigid analytic variety, smooth of dimension
d
over
L
. Then

(1)

There exists a canonical trace map
{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}:\,H_{c}^{d}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})\to L
.

(2)

For all coherent sheaves
{\mathscr{F}}
on
X
, the pairings

\displaystyle H_{c}^{i}(X,{\mathscr{F}})\times{\rm Ext}^{d-i}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X}}({\mathscr{F}},\Omega_{X}^{d})\to H_{c}^{d}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})\xrightarrow{{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}}L

\displaystyle H^{i}(X,{\mathscr{F}})\times{\rm Ext}^{d-i}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X},c}({\mathscr{F}},\Omega_{X}^{d})\to H_{c}^{d}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})\xrightarrow{{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}}L

induce perfect dualities, i.e., we have the induced isomorphisms:

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{d-i}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X}}({\mathscr{F}},\Omega_{X}^{d})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L}(H_{c}^{i}(X,{\mathscr{F}}),L)

and

\displaystyle H_{c}^{i}(X,{\mathscr{F}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L}({\rm Ext}^{d-i}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X}}({\mathscr{F}},\Omega_{X}^{d}),L),

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{d-i}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X},c}({\mathscr{F}},\Omega_{X}^{d})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L}(H^{i}(X,{\mathscr{F}}),L)

and

\displaystyle H^{i}(X,{\mathscr{F}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L}({\rm Ext}^{d-i}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X},c}({\mathscr{F}},\Omega_{X}^{d}),L).

This result was proved for smooth Stein varieties over
C
by Chiarellotto in
[
8
]
. This was then extended by van der Put to include proper rigid analytic varieties over
L
in
[
36
]
. A different proof for smooth Stein varieties over
L
was given by Beyer in
[
4
]
. In the following we will recall the strategy of the proof of
[
8
]
,
[
36
]
and in the next section we will reformulate their main results in the condensed setting.

(i)
Duality for the affine space.
Let
{\mathbb{A}}^{d}_{L}
be the rigid affine space of dimension
d
over
L
. Cover
{\mathbb{A}}^{d}_{L}
with the discs:

D_{j}:=\{x=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{d})\in{\mathbb{A}}^{d}_{L}\;|\;|x_{i}|\leq\frac{1}{p^{j}}\text{ for }1\leq i\leq d\}

and recall that the compactly supported cohomology of
\Omega^{d}
can be computed using the distinguished triangle:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{c}({\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{d},\Omega^{d})\to{\rm R}\Gamma({\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{d},\Omega^{d})\to\operatorname{colim}_{j}{\rm R}\Gamma({\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{d}\setminus D_{j},\Omega^{d}).

(5.2)

Lemma 5.3
.

We have
H^{i}_{c}({\mathbb{A}}^{d}_{L},\Omega^{d})=0
for
i\neq d
and for
i=d
:

H^{d}_{c}({\mathbb{A}}^{d}_{L},\Omega^{d})\simeq\{\sum_{\nu>0}\frac{a_{\nu}}{x^{\nu}}dx_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dx_{d}\;|\;\exists j\in{\bf N}\text{ such that }\lim_{|\nu|\to\infty}p^{-j|\nu|}|a_{\nu}|=0\},

where
\nu=(\nu_{1},\cdots,\nu_{d})\in{\bf Z}^{d}
and
x^{\nu}=x_{1}^{\nu_{1}}x_{2}^{\nu_{2}}\cdots x_{d}^{\nu_{d}}
.

Proof.

The result follows from the long exact sequence (
5.2
) using that we have

H^{i}({\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{d},\Omega^{d})=0
, for
i\neq 0
, and

H^{i}({\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{d}\setminus D_{j},\Omega^{d})\simeq\begin{cases}H^{0}({\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{d},\Omega^{d})&\text{ if }i=0,\\
\{\sum_{\nu>0}\frac{a_{\nu}}{x^{\nu}}dx_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dx_{d}\;|\;\lim_{|\nu|\to\infty}p^{-j|\nu|}|a_{\nu}|=0\}&\text{ if }i=d-1,\\
0&\text{ if }i\neq 0,d-1.\end{cases}

To prove the latter formula, consider the admissible covering of
{\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{d}\setminus D_{j}
by the open subsets
\{U_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq d}
where

U_{i}:=\{x=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{d})\in{\mathbb{A}}^{d}_{L}\;|\;|x_{i}|>\frac{1}{p^{j}}\},\quad\text{ for }i\in\{1,\cdots,d\},

and use the associated Čech cohomology.
∎

We define the trace map for the affine space as:

\displaystyle{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}:\qquad H^{d}_{c}({\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{d},\Omega^{d})\quad

\displaystyle\to L

(5.4)

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu>0}\frac{a_{\nu}}{x^{\nu}}dx

\displaystyle\mapsto a_{(1,\dots,1)}

where
\nu=(\nu_{1},\cdots,\nu_{d})\in{\bf Z}^{d}
,
x^{\nu}=x_{1}^{\nu_{1}}x_{2}^{\nu_{2}}\cdots x_{d}^{\nu_{d}}
and
dx:=dx_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dx_{d}
.

Proposition 5.5
.

[
36
, (2.5) and (2.6)]

Theorem
5.1
is true for
X={\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{d}
.

Proof.

The trace map is given by the formula (
5.4
).

First assume that
{\mathscr{F}}={\mathscr{O}}_{X}
. For
i\neq d
, both terms of the product are zero, so the pairing is perfect. For
i=d
, the pairing is given by

\displaystyle H_{c}^{d}(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X})

\displaystyle\times H^{0}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})\to L

\displaystyle(\sum_{\nu>0}\frac{a_{\nu}}{x^{\nu}}

\displaystyle,\sum_{\nu\geq 0}b_{\nu}x^{\nu}dx)\mapsto\sum_{\nu\geq 0}a_{\nu+1}b_{\nu}

and it induces a perfect duality. This proves the proposition for
{\mathscr{F}}={\mathscr{O}}_{X}
and we can deduce from it the result, first, for
{\mathscr{F}}={\mathscr{O}}_{X}^{m}
and then for a general coherent sheaf.
∎

(ii)
Duality for Stein varieties.

We can now deduce from the previous proposition the duality result for general smooth Stein varieties. Assume that
X
is Stein and choose a closed immersion
i:X\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{A}}^{N}_{L}
. We have the following classical results:

Lemma 5.6
.

[
8
, (4.15)]

There is a canonical quasi-isomorphism
\Omega_{X}^{d}[d-N]\simeq{\mathrm{R}}i^{!}\Omega_{{\mathbb{A}}^{N}_{C}}^{N}
.

Corollary 5.7
.

(1)

There is a canonical quasi-isomorphism
i_{*}\Omega_{X}^{d}[d-N]\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{N}}}(i_{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{X},\Omega_{{\mathbb{A}}^{N}_{C}}^{N})
.

(2)

For all coherent sheaves
{\mathscr{F}}
on
X
, there is a canonical isomorphism
H_{c}^{i}(X,{\mathscr{F}})\xrightarrow{\sim}H_{c}^{i}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{N},i_{*}{\mathscr{F}})
, for all
i\geq 0
.

(3)

For all coherent sheaves
{\mathscr{F}}
on
X
, there is a canonical quasi-isomorphism

{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X}}({\mathscr{F}},\Omega_{X}^{d})[d-N]\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathbb{A}}^{N}_{C}}}(i_{*}{\mathscr{F}},\Omega_{{\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{N}}^{N}).

Proof.

The first claim follows from Lemma
5.6
using the fact that
i^{!}
is right adjoint to
i_{*}
.

To prove the claim (2), note that the result is true for the usual cohomology (without compact support). We deduce the isomorphism for the compactly supported cohomology using that for each open affinoid
V
of
X
, we can find a finite set of open affinoids
Z_{1},\cdots,Z_{n}
in the affine space such that
V
is the intersection of the
Z_{i}
’s with
X
(see
[
8
, (4.17)]
).

For the third claim, consider an injective resolution
{\mathscr{I}}_{\bullet}
of
\Omega_{{\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{N}}^{N}
. Then

{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X}}(i_{*}{\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{I}}^{\bullet})\simeq{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathbb{A}}^{N}_{C}}}({\mathscr{F}},i^{!}{\mathscr{I}}^{\bullet}).

We conclude using that the functor
i^{!}
sends injectives to injectives and applying Lemma
5.6
.
∎

We define the trace map for a smooth Stein variety
X
as the composition:

\displaystyle H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d}_{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{d}_{c}({\mathbb{A}}^{N}_{C},

\displaystyle i_{*}\Omega_{X}^{d})\simeq{\rm Ext}^{N}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X},c}(i_{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{X},\Omega_{{\mathbb{A}}^{N}_{C}}^{N})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm{Hom}}_{L}(H^{0}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{N},i_{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{X}),L)\to L,

where the first and second isomorphisms follow from the previous lemma and the third one from the duality for the affine space.

Lemma 5.8
.

(
[
36
, 3.5, 3.7]
)

Let
X
be a smooth Stein variety over
L
, of dimension
d
. Then,

(1)

The trace map does not depend of the chosen closed immersion
X\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{A}}^{N}_{L}
.

(2)

If
X\to Y
is an open immersion of smooth Stein varieties over
L
of dimension
d
, the composition

H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})\to H^{d}_{c}(Y,\Omega^{d})\xrightarrow{{\rm tr}_{{\rm coh},Y}}L

is equal to
{\rm tr}_{{\rm coh},X}:H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})\to L
.

Proposition 5.9
.

The theorem
5.1
is true for smooth Stein varieties.

Proof.

Let
X
be a smooth Stein variety. Our theorem
follows from the duality for the affine space (Proposition
5.5
) using the isomorphisms from Corollary
5.7
:

\displaystyle H_{c}^{i}(X,{\mathscr{F}})\xrightarrow{\sim}H_{c}^{i}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{N},i_{*}{\mathscr{F}})\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X}}^{d-i}({\mathscr{F}},\Omega^{d}_{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{N}}}^{N-i}(i_{*}{\mathscr{F}},\Omega^{N}_{{\mathbb{A}}^{N}_{C}})

\displaystyle H^{i}(X,{\mathscr{F}})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{i}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{N},i_{*}{\mathscr{F}})\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X},c}^{d-i}({\mathscr{F}},\Omega^{d}_{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{N}},c}^{N-i}(i_{*}{\mathscr{F}},\Omega^{N}_{{\mathbb{A}}^{N}_{C}}).

∎

In particular, we obtain:

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
X
be a smooth Stein variety of dimension
d
over
L
and let
{\mathscr{F}}
be a locally free sheaf on
X
. Then
H^{i}_{c}(X,{\mathscr{F}})=0
if
i\neq d
.

5.1.2.
Overconvergent setting

In the dagger case the coherent duality was studied by Grosse-Klönne. We will review it briefly.

Theorem 5.11
.

[
21
, Th. 4.4]

Let
X
be smooth dagger affinoid of dimension
d
over
L
. Then

(1)

There exists a canonical trace map
{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}:\,H_{c}^{d}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})\to L
.

(2)

For all coherent sheaves
{\mathscr{F}}
on
X
, the pairings

\displaystyle H_{c}^{i}(X,{\mathscr{F}})\times{\rm Ext}^{d-i}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X}}({\mathscr{F}},\Omega_{X}^{d})\to H_{c}^{d}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})\xrightarrow{{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}}L

\displaystyle H^{i}(X,{\mathscr{F}})\times{\rm Ext}^{d-i}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X},c}({\mathscr{F}},\Omega_{X}^{d})\to H_{c}^{d}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})\xrightarrow{{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}}L

induce perfect dualities, i.e., we have the induced isomorphisms of
L
-vector spaces:

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{d-i}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X}}({\mathscr{F}},\Omega_{X}^{d})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L}(H_{c}^{i}(X,{\mathscr{F}}),L)

and

\displaystyle H_{c}^{i}(X,{\mathscr{F}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L}({\rm Ext}^{d-i}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X}}({\mathscr{F}},\Omega_{X}^{d}),L),

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{d-i}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X},c}({\mathscr{F}},\Omega_{X}^{d})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L}(H^{i}(X,{\mathscr{F}}),L)

and

\displaystyle H^{i}(X,{\mathscr{F}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L}({\rm Ext}^{d-i}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X},c}({\mathscr{F}},\Omega_{X}^{d}),L).

The proof is similar to the one in the rigid case. We will first prove the result for
X:={\rm Sp}(L[X_{1},\cdots,X_{d}]^{\dagger})
and then consider a closed immersion
i:X\hookrightarrow{\rm Sp}(L[X_{1},\cdots,X_{d}]^{\dagger})
to deduce the duality for a general dagger affinoid
X
.

(i)
Duality for
X:={\rm Sp}(L[X_{1},\cdots,X_{d}]^{\dagger})
.
As in the affine case, we can easily compute the compactly supported cohomology for the sheaf of differentials
\Omega^{d}
. This will allow us to define the trace map.

Lemma 5.12
.

We have
H^{i}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})=0
if
i\neq d
and for
i=d
we have

H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})\simeq\{\sum_{\nu>0}\frac{a_{\nu}}{x^{\nu}}dx_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dx_{d}\;|\;\lim_{|\nu|\to\infty}\delta^{|\nu|}|a_{\nu}|=0\text{ for all }\delta>1\}

where
\nu=(\nu_{1},\cdots,\nu_{d})\in{\bf Z}^{d}
and
x^{\nu}=x_{1}^{\nu_{1}}x_{2}^{\nu_{2}}\cdots x_{d}^{\nu_{d}}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the one of Lemma
5.3
: if we write
X_{\delta}:={\rm Sp}(L[\delta^{-1}X_{1},\cdots,\delta^{-1}X_{d}])
, for
\delta>1
, then we can use the long exact sequence:

\cdots\to\operatorname{colim}_{\delta}H^{i-1}(X_{\delta}\setminus\widehat{X},\Omega^{d})\to H^{i}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})\to\operatorname{colim}_{\delta}H^{i}(X_{\delta},\Omega^{d})\to\operatorname{colim}_{\delta}H^{i-1}(X_{\delta}\setminus\widehat{X},\Omega^{d})\to\cdots

to compute
H^{i}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})
(using that
H^{i}(X_{\delta},\Omega^{d})=0
for
i\neq 0
).
∎

We define the trace map for
X={\rm Sp}(L[X_{1},\cdots,X_{d}]^{\dagger})
as

\displaystyle{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}:\qquad H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})\quad

\displaystyle\to L,

(5.13)

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu>0}\frac{a_{\nu}}{x^{\nu}}dx

\displaystyle\mapsto a_{(1,\dots,1)},

where
\nu=(\nu_{1},\cdots,\nu_{d})\in{\bf Z}^{d}
,
x^{\nu}=x_{1}^{\nu_{1}}x_{2}^{\nu_{2}}\cdots x_{d}^{\nu_{d}}
and
dx:=dx_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dx_{d}
.

Proposition 5.14
.

Theorem
5.11
is true for
X={\rm Sp}(L[X_{1},\cdots,X_{d}]^{\dagger})
.

Proof.

The trace map is given by the formula (
5.13
). The proof of the duality is the same as the one of Proposition
5.5
: it is enough to prove the result for
{\mathscr{F}}={\mathscr{O}}_{X}
and in that case the pairing can be written explicitly.
∎

(ii)
Duality for dagger affinoids.
Let
X
be a smooth dagger affinoid of dimension
d
. By definition, we can find a closed immersion
i
of
X
into the Tate algebra
Y:={\rm Sp}(L[X_{1},\cdots,X_{d}]^{\dagger})
. All the results concerning the closed immersion
i
from Section
5.1.1
are still true in this case (but simpler, since the immersion that we have now preserves the dimension). We define the trace map for
X
as the composition:

\displaystyle H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d}_{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{d}_{c}(Y,

\displaystyle i_{*}\Omega_{X}^{d})\simeq{\rm Ext}^{d}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{X},c}(i_{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{X},\Omega_{Y}^{d})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm{Hom}}_{L}(H^{0}(Y,i_{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{Y}),L)\to L.

Then, Theorem
5.11
follows from Proposition
5.14
the same way as Proposition
5.9
follows from Proposition
5.5
.

In particular, we obtain:

Proposition 5.15
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger affinoid of dimension
d
over
L
, and let
{\mathscr{F}}
be a locally free sheaf on
X
. Then
H^{i}_{c}(X,{\mathscr{F}})=0
if
i\neq d
.

Moreover, as in the rigid case, we have that the trace map is compatible with open immersions of dagger affinoids:

Lemma 5.16
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger affinoid over
L
, of dimension
d
. Then,

(1)

The trace map does not depend of the chosen closed immersion
X\hookrightarrow{\rm Sp}(L[X_{1},\cdots,X_{d}]^{\dagger})
.

(2)

If
X\to Y
is an open immersion of dagger smooth affinoids over
L
of dimension
d
, the composition

H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})\to H^{d}_{c}(Y,\Omega^{d})\xrightarrow{{\rm tr}_{{\rm coh},Y}}L

is equal to
{\rm tr}_{{\rm coh},X}:H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})\to L
.

Proof.

The proof can be reduced to the Stein case, i.e., to Lemma
5.8
.
∎

Remark 5.17
.

(
Topological issues.
)
All the terms in Theorem
5.1
and Theorem
5.11
are equipped with a natural topology and the dualities are topological. See
[
8
, Th. 4.21]
for statements and discussions concerning these facts.

5.2.
Coherent duality: condensed approach

The classical coherent duality results surveyed above can be paraphrased in the condensed mathematics language.

Corollary 5.18
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid analytic variety over
L
, of dimension
d
. Then:

(1)

There are trace maps in
L_{\Box}
and
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})
, respectively:

\operatorname{tr}_{\rm coh}:H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d}_{X})\to L,\quad{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})[d]\to L.

They are compatible with open immersions.

(2)

If
X
is Stein or proper, the pairing

H^{i}(X,\Omega^{j}_{X})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}_{L}H^{d-i}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d-j}_{X})\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d}_{X})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\operatorname{tr}_{\rm coh}}}L

is perfect, i.e., it yields the quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})

\displaystyle\Omega^{j}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d-j}_{X}),L),\quad\Omega^{j}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d-j}_{X}),L),

(5.19)

\displaystyle H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{j}_{X})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(\Omega^{d-j}(X),L).

(3)

The pairing

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X,\Omega^{j}_{X})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}_{L}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{d-j}_{X})\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{d}_{X})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\operatorname{tr}_{\rm coh}}}L[-d]

is perfect, i.e., it yields the quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X,\Omega^{j}_{X})\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{d-j}_{X})[d],L)

Proof.

For claim (1), assume first that
X
is Stein. Recall that the locally convex spaces
H^{i}_{c}(X,\Omega^{j})
and
H^{i}(X,\Omega^{j})
are of compact type and nuclear Fréchet (both in the classical sense), respectively. We can see them inside the category of solid
L
-modules. Then the claim (1) follows from Proposition
5.9
. For a general smooth and partially proper
X
take an admissible covering
\{U_{i}\}
,
i\in I
, of
X
such that the
U_{i}
’s are Stein. We have the exact sequence:

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i,j\in I}H^{d}_{c}(U_{i}\cap U_{j},\Omega^{d})\to\bigoplus_{i\in I}H_{c}^{d}(U_{i},\Omega^{d})\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})\to 0.

We define the trace map
{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}:H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})\to L

using the above exact sequence and the fact that the construction of the coherent trace for Stein varieties is compatible with the open immersions
U_{i}\cap U_{j}\to U_{i}
(Lemma
5.8
). The derived trace
{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})\to L
is defined as the composition

{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\tau_{\leq d}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})[-d]\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}}}L[-d].

For the last statement of claim (1) we have the following:

Lemma 5.20
.

Let
j:X\to Y
be an open immersion of smooth partially proper rigid analytic varieties of dimension
d
over
L
. The coherent trace map is compatible with
j_{*}
, i.e.,
the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{{\rm coh},X}}
\textstyle{H^{d}_{c}(Y,\Omega_{Y}^{d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{{\rm coh},Y}}
\textstyle{L.}

Proof.

If
X,Y
are Stein this follows from Lemma
5.8
.

For a general partially proper
Y
, take an admissible covering
\{U_{i}\}
of
Y
by Stein varieties. Recall that the trace map
\operatorname{tr}_{Y}:H^{d}_{c}(Y,\Omega_{Y}^{d})\to L
comes from the maps
\operatorname{tr}_{U_{i}}:H^{d}_{c}(U_{i},\Omega_{U_{i}}^{d})\to L
. Consider the diagram:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 46.39876pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr&&&\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-46.39876pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigoplus_{i}H^{d}_{c}(U_{i}\times_{Y}X,\Omega^{d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.90524pt\raise-49.07085pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.65001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{X}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 96.60327pt\raise-57.81837pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 146.80779pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H^{d}_{c}(Y,\Omega^{d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 171.93591pt\raise-49.07085pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.65001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{Y}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 171.93591pt\raise-57.81837pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern
