0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 89.67203pt\raise-65.15169pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern 165.00467pt\raise-65.15169pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 252.85548pt\raise-65.15169pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

We want to show that the central square is commutative. Since the maps from the direct sums to
H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})
and
H^{d}_{c}(Y,\Omega^{d})
are surjective, it suffices to show that the upper and outer trapezoids as well as the right and left triangles are commutative.

The right triangle is commutative by definition of the trace map for
Y
; for the upper trapezoid, it is by definition of the pushforward map; for the left triangle and the outer trapezoid this follows from the previous point (since
U_{i}\times_{Y}X
is Stein).
∎

For the claim (2), we again use the cited proposition and the fact that
we have

{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{2d-i}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d-j}_{X}),L)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{2d-i}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d-j}_{X}),L)

because
H^{i}_{c}(X,\Omega^{j})
is of compact type.

For claim (3), we cover
X
with Stein varieties
\{X_{i}\},i\in I
, and consider the associated Čech hypercovering
Y_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
of
X
. Then we compute in

{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})

\displaystyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}(X,\Omega^{j})

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\lim_{i}{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}(Y_{i},\Omega^{j})\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\lim_{i}{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}_{c}(Y_{i},\Omega^{d-j})[d],L)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(\operatorname{colim}_{i}{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}_{c}(Y_{i},\Omega^{d-j})[d],L)\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d-j})[d],L),

as wanted. Here, the second quasi-isomorphism follows from the duality for Stein varieties stated in claim (2).

∎

We have an analogous duality result in the overconvergent setting:

Corollary 5.21
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
L
, of dimension
d
. Then:

(1)

There is a trace map of solid
L
-modules

\operatorname{tr}_{\rm coh}:H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d}_{X})\to L.

(2)

If
X
is an affinoid then the pairing

H^{i}(X,\Omega^{j})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}_{L}H^{2d-i}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d-j}_{X})\to H^{2d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d}_{X})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\operatorname{tr}_{\rm coh}}}L

is perfect, i.e., it yields the quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})

\displaystyle H^{i}_{c}(X,\Omega^{j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{2d-i}(X,\Omega^{d-j}_{X}),L),\quad H^{i}_{c}(X,\Omega^{j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{2d-i}(X,\Omega^{d-j}_{X}),L),

(5.22)

\displaystyle H^{i}(X,\Omega^{j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{2d-i}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d-j}_{X}),L).

(3)

If
X
is quasi-compact, the pairing

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X,\Omega^{j}_{X})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}_{L}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{d-j}_{X})\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{d}_{X})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\operatorname{tr}_{\rm coh}}}L[-d]

is perfect, i.e., it yields the quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{j}_{X})\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X,\Omega^{d-j}_{X})[d],L)

Proof.

The proof of (1) and (2) is the mirror image of the proof of Corollary
5.18
, using Lemma
5.16
and the fact that if
X
is an affinoid then the locally convex spaces
H^{i}(X,\Omega^{j})
and
H^{i}_{c}(X,\Omega^{j})
are of compact type and nuclear Fréchet (both in the classical sense), respectively.

For claim (3), we cover
X
with affinoid dagger varieties
\{X_{i}\},i\in I
, and consider the associated Čech hypercovering
Y_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
of
X
. Then we compute in

{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})

\displaystyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}_{c}(X,\Omega^{j})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{i}{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}}(Y_{i},\Omega^{j})\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{i}{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}(Y_{i},\Omega^{d-j})[d],L)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\lim_{i}{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}(Y_{i},\Omega^{d-j})[d],L)\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}(X,\Omega^{d-j})[d],L),

as wanted. Here, the second quasi-isomorphism follows from the duality for affinoid dagger varieties stated in claim (2) and the third quasi-isomorphism from the fact that the limits and colimits are finite.
∎

5.3.
de Rham dualities

We state the dualities satisfied by de Rham and Hyodo-Kato cohomologies of smooth partially proper rigid analytic varieties and smooth dagger affinoids. We deduce the de Rham duality from coherent duality and the Hyodo-Kato duality is obtained from the de Rham duality using the Hyodo-Kato isomorphism.

5.3.1.
Rigid analytic setting

We can now prove the duality result satisfied by the de Rham cohomology:

Theorem 5.23
.

(
de Rham duality
)

Let
L=K,C
and
X
be a smooth partially proper variety of dimension
d
over
L
. Then:

(1)

There are natural trace maps in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})
and
L_{\Box}
, respectively:

{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}:\,{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d]\to L,\quad{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}:\,H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\to L.

(2)

The pairing

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}_{L}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d]\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d]\xrightarrow{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}}L

is a perfect duality, i.e., we have induced quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d],L).

(3)

The pairing

H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}_{L}H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\to H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\xrightarrow{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}}L

is a perfect duality of solid
L
-vector spaces, i.e., we have induced isomorphisms in
L_{\Box}

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X),L),

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),L).

Proof.

In the case
X
is Stein,
the trace map
{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}:H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\to L
can be induced by the coherent trace map
{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}:H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})\to L
from
Corollary
5.18
, noting that
{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}(\partial)=0
where
\partial:H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d-1})\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})
(see
[
4
, Lemma 2.1.4]
). Its derived version
{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d]\to L
is defined as the composition

{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\tau_{\leq 2d}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d]\to H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm tr}_{\rm dR}}}L.

In the case of general partially proper
X
we reduce to the Stein case as in the proof of Corollary
5.18
.

In the case
X
is Stein, claim (2) follows immediately from the sequence representing
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)
:

{\mathscr{O}}(X)\to\Omega^{1}(X)\to\cdots\to\Omega^{d}(X),

(5.24)

the sequence (
3.13
) representing
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)
, and derived Serre duality (
5.19
). For a general partially proper
X
, we cover it with Stein varieties
\{X_{i}\},i\in I
, and consider the associated Čech hypercovering
Y_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
of
X
. Then we compute in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})

\displaystyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\lim_{i}{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}_{\operatorname{dR}}(Y_{i})\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\lim_{i}{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(Y_{i})[2d],L)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(\operatorname{colim}_{i}{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(Y_{i})[2d],L)\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d],L),

as wanted. Here, the second quasi-isomorphism follows from the duality for Stein varieties.

The first isomorphism in claim (3) follows from claim (2) by taking cohomology. We use here the fact that
\operatorname{Ext}^{j}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{m}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X),L)=0
, for
j\geq 1
, because
H^{m}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)
is of compact type (in fact a countable direct sum of finite rank vector spaces). The second isomorphism follows from the computation:

\displaystyle{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),L)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X),L),L)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X),

where the first isomorphism was just proven and the second one follows from reflexivity of
H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)
.
∎

Lemma 5.25
.

Let
j:X\to Y
be an open immersion of smooth partially proper rigid analytic varieties of dimension
d
over
L
. The de Rham trace map is compatible with
j_{*}
, i.e.,
the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR},X}}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR},Y}}
\textstyle{L.}

Proof.

This follows immediately from the definition of the de Rham trace map and an analogous claim for coherent traces (see Lemma
5.20
).
∎

Corollary 5.26
.

(
Filtered de Rham duality
)

Let
L=K,C
and
X
be a smooth partially proper variety of dimension
d
over
L
.
Let
r,r^{\prime}\in{\bf N},r+r^{\prime}=d
. The pairing in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})

({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)/F^{r^{\prime}})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}_{L}F^{r+1}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d]\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d]\xrightarrow{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}}L

is a perfect duality, i.e., we have induced quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)/F^{r^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(F^{r+1}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d],L),

\displaystyle F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)/F^{r+1}[2d],L).

Proof.

Assume first that
X
is Stein.
We compute:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X),L)

\displaystyle\simeq(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{r^{\prime}})^{*}\leftarrow H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{r^{\prime}+1})^{*}\leftarrow\cdots\leftarrow H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})^{*})[-d-r^{\prime}]

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathscr{O}}(X)\to\Omega^{1}(X)\to\cdots\to\Omega^{r}(X)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)/F^{r+1}.

We wrote here
(-)^{*}
for
{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(-,L)
and used the fact that

{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{i}),L)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{i}),L)

because the cohomology groups with compact support are of compact type.
The second quasi-isomorphism follows from Corollary
5.18
.

The case of
X
partially proper follows from the Stein case by a limit/colimit argument as in the proof of Theorem
5.23
.
∎

The duality for
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology has a different form. For
r\geq d
, a natural trace map in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})
can be defined by the composition

{\rm tr}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}:\quad F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})[2d]\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})\otimes_{K}^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}F^{r-d}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}\otimes{\rm Id}}}F^{r-d}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}.

Corollary 5.27
.

(
Filtered
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-duality
)
Let
X
be a smooth partially proper variety of dimension
d
over
K
,
r,r^{\prime}\geq d
and
s=r+r^{\prime}-d
. Then we have a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})
:

F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}}(F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

Proof.

Assume first that
X
is Stein.
This follows from the computation:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}}(F^{r^{\prime}}

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq{\rm{Hom}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}}(F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

(5.28)

\displaystyle\simeq(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})^{*}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}t^{r}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d-1})^{*}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}t^{r-1}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\cdots\to H^{d}_{c}(X,{\mathscr{O}})^{*}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}t^{r-d}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})[-d],

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathscr{O}}(X)\otimes^{\Box}_{K}t^{r}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\Omega^{1}(X)\otimes^{\Box}_{K}t^{r-1}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\cdots\to\Omega^{d}(X)\otimes^{\Box}_{K}t^{r-d}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}

\displaystyle\simeq F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

We used here the quasi-isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}}(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{i})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}_{K}t^{r^{\prime}-i}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}},F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{i})^{*}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}t^{r-i}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}},

which hold because we have:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}}(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{i})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}_{K}t^{r^{\prime}-i}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}},t^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{K_{\Box}}(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{i}),t^{s-r^{\prime}+i}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{K_{\Box}}(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{i}),K)\otimes_{K}^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}F^{s-r^{\prime}+i}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}

\displaystyle\simeq H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{i})^{*}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}t^{r-d+i}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}.

Here, the first quasi-isomorphism is by adjunction; the second quasi-isomorphism follows from the fact that
H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{i})
is of compact type,
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
is Fréchet, and we have
[
31
, Th. 3.40]
.

The case of
X
partially proper follows from the Stein case by a limit/colimit argument as in the proof of Theorem
5.23
.
∎

5.3.2.
Overconvergent setting

By the same arguments as in the proof of Theorem
5.23
we deduce the Poincaré duality for de Rham cohomology:

Theorem 5.29
.

(de Rham duality)

Let
L=K,C
and
X
be a smooth dagger variety of dimension
d
over
L
. Then:

(1)

There are natural trace maps in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})
and
L_{\Box}
, respectively:

{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}:\,{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d]\to L,\quad{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}:\,H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\to L.

(2)

If
X
is quasi-compact, the pairing

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}_{L}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d]\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d]\xrightarrow{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}}L

is a perfect duality, i.e., we have an induced quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d],L).

(3)

If
X
is quasi-compact,
the pairing

H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}_{L}H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\to H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\xrightarrow{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}}L

is a perfect duality of solid
L
-vector spaces, i.e., we have an induced quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X),L),

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),L).

Proof.

In the case
X
is affinoid,
the trace map
{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}:H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\to L
can be induced by the coherent trace map
{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}:H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})\to L
from
Corollary
5.21
, noting that
{\rm tr}_{\rm coh}(\partial)=0
where
\partial:H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d-1})\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega_{X}^{d})
(reduce to the Stein case and use
[
4
, Lemma 2.1.4]
). Its derived version
{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d]\to L
is defined as the composition

{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\tau_{\leq 2d}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d]\to H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm tr}_{\rm dR}}}L.

In the case of general smooth
X
we reduce to the affinoid case as in the proof of Corollary
5.21
.

In the case
X
is affinoid, claim (2) follows immediately from the sequence representing
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)
:

{\mathscr{O}}(X)\to\Omega^{1}(X)\to\cdots\to\Omega^{d}(X),

the analog of the sequence (
3.13
) representing
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)
, and derived Serre duality (
5.19
). For a general quasi-compact
X
, we cover it with
dagger affinoid varieties
\{X_{i}\},i\in I
, and consider the associated Čech hypercovering
Y_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
of
X
. Then we compute in
{\mathscr{D}}(L_{\Box})

\displaystyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{i}{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(Y_{i})\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{i}{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}_{\operatorname{dR}}(Y_{i})[2d],L)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\lim_{i}{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}_{\operatorname{dR}}(Y_{i})[2d],L)\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)[2d],L),

as wanted. Here, the second quasi-isomorphism follows from the duality for affinoid varieties and the third quasi-isomorphism from the fact that limits/colimits are finite.

The first isomorphism in claim (3) follows from claim (2) by taking cohomology. We use here the fact that
\operatorname{Ext}^{j}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{m}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),L)=0
, for
j\geq 1
, because
H^{m}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)
is of finite rank. The second isomorphism follows from the computation:

\displaystyle{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X),L)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}{\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}({\rm{Hom}}_{L_{\Box}}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),L),L)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),

where the first isomorphism was just proven and the second one follows from (trivial) reflexivity of
H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)
.
∎

5.4.
Hyodo-Kato duality

Using the Hyodo-Kato isomorphisms we can deduce Poincaré duality for the Hyodo-Kato cohomology of a smooth partially proper rigid analytic variety or a smooth dagger affinoid over
L=K,C
from the one for de Rham cohomology.

5.4.1.
Rigid analytic setting

Theorem 5.30
.

Let
L=K,C
and
X
be a smooth partially proper variety of dimension
d
over
L
. Set
F_{L}=F,F^{\rm nr},\breve{F}
and
*=-,\breve{F}
. Then:

(1)

There is a natural trace map in the category of solid
(\varphi,N)
-modules over
F_{L}

{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{HK}}:\,H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\to F_{L}\{-d\},

which is compatible with the de Rham trace map via the Hyodo-Kato morphism and Galois equivariant in the case
L=C
and
X
is defined over
K
.

(2)

The pairing of solid
(\varphi,N)
-modules over
F_{L}

H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X)\otimes^{\Box}_{F_{L}}H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\to H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\xrightarrow{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{HK}}}F_{L}\{-d\}

is a perfect duality, i.e., we have induced isomorphisms of solid
(\varphi,N)
-modules over
F_{L}

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{F_{L,\Box}}(H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X),F_{L}\{-d\}),

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{F_{L,\Box}}(H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X),F_{L}\{-d\}).

These isomorphisms are Galois equivariant if
L=C
and
X
is defined over
K
.

Proof.

Assume first that we can define a trace map
{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{HK}}:H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\to F_{L}\{-d\}

such that the following diagram commutes:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 61.73595pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-61.73595pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 166.42784pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 89.81584pt\raise-33.33725pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 148.6543pt\raise-27.65392pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62224pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 180.30804pt\raise-33.33725pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 180.30804pt\raise-33.33725pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

(5.31)

Then, since the map from the bottom row is a perfect pairing, via the Hyodo-Kato isomorphisms, the top row is a perfect pairing as well and we obtain the duality for the Hyodo-Kato cohomology.

Hence it suffices to prove claim (1). We will show that the de Rham trace map factorises through
F_{L}
when restricted to
H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)
yielding the Hyodo-Kato trace map with the required properties. We may assume that
X
is connected. We start with the case
L=C
and
*=\breve{F}
.
Consider the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{L}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{L})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{L})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{\breve{F}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{\breve{F}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\breve{F}\{-d\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(5.32)

Here
U\subset X
is an open ball of dimension
d
inside
X
. The bottom de Rham trace map is compatible with Frobenius as can be easily seen by passing to the affine space and using formula (
5.4
).
The above diagram shows that the top horizontal composition factors through
\breve{F}
, defining the trace map
{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{HK}}:H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}(X)\to\breve{F}\{-d\}
, which is clearly compatible with the de Rham trace map and Frobenius. Moreover, the monodromy on

H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}(X)
is trivial (since it is so on
H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F},c}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{\breve{F}})
) hence the trace map
{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{HK}}
is compatible with monodromy.
Same for the residual Galois action.

The same argument works for
L=K
(and
F_{L}=F
). For
L=C
and
F_{L}=F^{\operatorname{nr}}
(and
*=-
) we need to modify it slightly. We replace the left column in diagram
5.32
with
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
-cohomology and then add the following commutative diagram to the bottom

\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},{F}^{\operatorname{nr}},c}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{L})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{L})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{L}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},{F}^{\operatorname{nr}},c}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{{\bf Q}_{p}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{{\bf Q}_{p}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\bf Q}_{p}\{-d\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Now we
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
-linearize the bottom row. This induces an isomorphism in the left column and our claim follows.
∎

Lemma 5.33
.

Let
j:X\to Y
be an open immersion of smooth partially proper rigid analytic varieties of dimension
d
over
L
. The Hyodo-Kato trace map is compatible with
j_{*}
, i.e.,
the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{HK},X}}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{HK},Y}}
\textstyle{F_{L}\{-d\}.}

Proof.

We will argue for
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
-cohomology; the argument for other
F_{L}
-cohomologies is analogous.
Consider the following diagram:

\textstyle{H^{2d}_{{\rm dR},c}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{X}}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{{\rm dR},c}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{Y}}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{X}}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{Y}}
\textstyle{F^{\rm nr}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{\rm nr}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C}

We want show that the central square commutes. For that, since the arrows from
F^{\rm nr}
to
C
are injective, it suffices to check the commutativity of the four trapezoids and the outer square. Commutativity is clear for the lower trapezoid. For the left and right trapezoids, it comes from the construction of the trace maps. For the upper one, it follows from the compatibility of the Hyodo-Kato morphism with the pushforward. It remains to show that the trace map for the de Rham cohomology is compatible with pushforward. But this is Lemma
5.25
.
∎

5.4.2.
Overconvergent setting

Theorem 5.34
.

Let
L=K,C
and
X
be a smooth dagger variety of dimension
d
over
L
. Set
F_{L}=F,F^{\rm nr},\breve{F}
and
*=-,\breve{F}
. Then:

(1)

There is a natural trace map in the category of solid
(\varphi,N)
-modules over
F_{L}

{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{HK}}:\,H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\to F_{L}\{-d\},

which is compatible with the de Rham trace map via the Hyodo-Kato morphism and Galois equivariant in the case
L=C
and
X
is defined over
K
.

(2)

If
X
is quasi-compact,
the pairing of solid
(\varphi,N)
-modules over
F_{L}

H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X)\otimes_{F_{L}}^{\Box}H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\to H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\xrightarrow{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{HK}}}F_{L}\{-d\}

is a perfect duality of solid
F_{L}
-vector spaces, i.e., we have induced isomorphisms of solid
(\varphi,N)
-modules over
F_{L}

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{F_{L},\Box}(H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X),L\{-d\}),

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{F_{L},\Box}(H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X),L\{-d\}).

These isomorphisms are Galois equivariant if
L=C
and
X
is defined over
K
.

Proof.

For the trace map in the affinoid case, we use dagger presentations and the trace map for Stein varieties.
We have an analog of Lemma
5.33
in this setting, i.e., for an open immersion of smooth dagger affinoids of dimension
d
over
K
. This can be easily seen from the definition of the Hyodo-Kato trace via a reduction to Lemma
5.33
. This yields, as in the proof of Corollary
5.21
, the trace map for general smooth dagger varieties.

For claim (2), note
first
that the following diagram commutes:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 61.73595pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-61.73595pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\otimes^{\Box}_{F_{L}}H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.66862pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.66862pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.73595pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 85.73595pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},*,c}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 114.0819pt\raise-16.66862pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 114.0819pt\raise-24.34723pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 143.06317pt\raise 5.65279pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.65279pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{HK}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 166.42784pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 166.42784pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F_{L}\{-d\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 186.71083pt\raise-26.00394pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-50.98827pt\raise-33.33725pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\otimes^{\Box}_{L}H^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 89.81584pt\raise-33.33725pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 89.81584pt\raise-33.33725pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 148.6543pt\raise-27.65392pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62224pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 180.30804pt\raise-33.33725pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 180.30804pt\raise-33.33725pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

That is, that the Hyodo-Kato and de Rham trace maps are compatible: which follows from the fact the de Rham trace map can also be defined using dagger presentations and Stein trace maps.
Then, since the map from the bottom row is a perfect pairing by Theorem
5.29
, via the Hyodo-Kato isomorphisms, the top row is a perfect pairing as well and we obtain the duality for the Hyodo-Kato cohomology.
∎

5.5.
Examples

We will compute the de Rham and Hyodo-Kato cohomologies of the affine space and the torus and check they satisfy the previous Poincaré duality results.

5.5.1.
Affine space

Let
X={\mathbb{A}}_{L}^{d}
be the affine dagger space over
L=K,C
. Recall that we have

H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\simeq\begin{cases}L\text{ for }i=0,\\
0\text{ for }i\neq 0,\end{cases}\text{ and }H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\simeq\begin{cases}F_{L},\text{ pour }i=0,\\
0,\text{ for }i\neq 0,\end{cases}

where
F_{C}=F^{\operatorname{nr}}
and
F_{K}=F
.

It follows that, by Poincaré duality, we have:

H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\simeq\begin{cases}0\text{ for }i\neq 2d,\\
L\text{ for }i=2d,\end{cases}\text{ and }H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)\simeq\begin{cases}0\text{ for }i\neq 2d,\\
F_{L}\{-d\}\text{ for }i=2d.\end{cases}

(5.35)

The monodromy on
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)
is trivial and so is the action of the Galois group
\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{K}/F^{\rm nr})
(in the case
L=C
).

We can however obtain this result more directly. Recall that
[
10
, proof of Th. 4.17]
, there exists a semistable weak formal scheme
{\mathscr{A}}^{d}
over
{\mathscr{O}}_{K}
such that
{\mathscr{A}}^{d}_{K}\simeq{\mathbb{A}}^{d}_{K}
. Moreover
there exist closed (resp. open) subschemes
Y_{s},s\in{\bf N},
(resp.
U_{s},s\in{\bf N}
) of the special fiber
Y:={\mathscr{A}}^{d}_{0}
such that

(1)

each
Y_{s}
is a finite union of irreducible components,

(2)

Y_{s}\subset U_{s}\subset Y_{s+1}
and their union is
Y
,

(3)

the tubes
\{\overline{U}_{s}:=]U_{s}[_{X}\},s\in{\bf N},
form a Stein covering of
{\mathscr{A}}^{d}_{K}
by closed discs.

We will call the covering
\{U_{s}\},s\in{\bf N},
a Stein covering of
Y
.
The schemes
U_{s},Y_{s}
inherit their log-structure from
Y
(which is canonically a log-scheme log-smooth over
k^{0}
). The log-schemes
Y_{s}
are not log-smooth (over
k^{0}
) but they are ideally log-smooth, i.e., they have a canonical idealized log-scheme structure and are ideally log-smooth for this structure.

\bullet

Computation of the de Rham cohomology with compact support.

We have the distinguished triangle

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X),\mbox{where }

(5.36)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X)\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{s}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X\setminus\overline{U}_{s}).

Since
X\setminus\overline{U}_{s}
is a punctured open disc, we have

H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X)\simeq\begin{cases}K&\mbox{ for }i=0,\\
K&\mbox{ for }i=2d-1,\\
0&\mbox{ for }i\neq 0,2d-1.\\
\end{cases}

The computation in (
5.35
) now easily follows from that fact and the distinguished triangle (
5.36
).

\bullet

Computation of the Hyodo-Kato cohomology with compact support.

Similarly, we can use the distinguished triangle (everything is over
F
now)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(\partial X),\mbox{where }

(5.37)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(\partial X)\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{s}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X\setminus U^{0}_{s}),

where
U^{0}_{s}
is the naive interior of
\overline{U}_{s}
, an open disc. Since
X\setminus\overline{U}^{0}_{s}
has a semistable model over
F
, we have:

(1)

The Hyodo-Kato morphism induces an isomorphism
\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(\partial X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F}K\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\partial X)
, for all
i\geq 0
, and so we obtain

H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(\partial X)\simeq\begin{cases}F&\mbox{ for }i=0,\\
F&\mbox{ for }i=2d-1,\\
0&\mbox{ for }i\neq 0,2d-1.\\
\end{cases}

(2)

Frobenius: we have the isomorphism
H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},c}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d})
and the Frobenius on the target group has slope
d
.

(3)

The monodromy is trivial on
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X\setminus U^{0}_{s})
(since the integral model has no loops) hence on
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(\partial X)
as well.

We then recover the results from (
5.35
) using (
5.37
).

5.5.2.
Torus

Let
X:={\mathscr{G}}_{m,K}^{d}
be a rigid analytic torus of dimension
d
. Recall that
H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{G}}_{m,K}^{d})
is a
K
-vector space of dimension
\binom{d}{i}
, generated by symbols
c_{1}^{\operatorname{dR}}(z_{j_{1}})\dots c_{1}^{\operatorname{dR}}(z_{j_{i}})
for
j_{1}<j_{2}<\dots<j_{i}
, where
c_{1}^{\operatorname{dR}}(z)=\frac{dz}{z}
is the de Rham Chern class. Similarly,
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{G}}_{m,K}^{d})
is a
F
-vector space of dimension
\binom{d}{i}
, generated by symbols
c_{1}^{\operatorname{HK}}(z_{j_{1}})\dots c_{1}^{\operatorname{HK}}(z_{j_{i}})
. The Chern classes
c_{1}^{\operatorname{dR}}(z)
and
c_{1}^{\operatorname{HK}}(z)
are compatible with the Hyodo-Kato isomorphism. Moreover
\varphi(c_{1}^{\operatorname{HK}}(z))=pc_{1}^{\operatorname{HK}}(z)
and
N(c_{1}^{\operatorname{HK}}(z))=0
.

Using Poincaré duality, we obtain that
H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}({\mathscr{G}}_{m,K}^{d})
is a
K
-vector space of dimension
\binom{d}{2d-i}
, generated by classes
(c_{1}^{\operatorname{dR}}(z_{j_{1}})\dots c_{1}^{\operatorname{dR}}(z_{j_{2d-i}}))^{*}
for
j_{1}<j_{2}<\dots<j_{2d-i}
.
Similarly,
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},c}({\mathscr{G}}_{m,K}^{d})
is a
F
-vector space of dimension
\binom{d}{2d-i}
, generated by classes
(c_{1}^{\operatorname{HK}}(z_{j_{1}})\dots c_{1}^{\operatorname{HK}}(z_{j_{2d-i}}))^{*}
. Monodromy acts trivially on these classes and Frobenius by multiplication by
p^{i-d}
.

6.
Geometric syntomic cohomology with compact support

In this section, we define geometric compactly supported syntomic cohomology for both smooth rigid analytic varieties and smooth dagger varieties. In each case, the previous recipes apply and similar properties are obtained. We then show that compactly supported syntomic cohomology can be computed in terms of compactly supported (twisted) Hyodo-Kato cohomology and (quotients of) compactly supported
{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}
-cohomology. In particular, this will allow us to compare the rigid definition with the dagger one for partially proper varieties.

6.1.
Rigid analytic setting

Recall (see
[
15
, Section 4.1]
) that geometric syntomic cohomology is defined by
\eta
-étale descent from the crystalline syntomic cohomology of Fontaine-Messing
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}({\mathscr{X}},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=[F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})\xrightarrow{p^{r}-\varphi}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})]
. By regarding the crystalline sheaves as condensed abelian groups, we can define the sheaf
{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(r)
on
{\rm Sm}_{C,\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}
as a sheaf with values in
{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}})
and set
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(r))
for
X\in{\rm Sm}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},C}
. Following the usual process, we define the compactly supported cohomology
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\in{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})
of a smooth variety
X
over
C
. It satisfies all the properties from Section
2.1
.

Recall that we have:

Theorem 6.1
.

[
16
, Corollary 5.5]

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
and
r\geq 0
. There is a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}})
:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\xrightarrow{\sim}[[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\xrightarrow{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+})/F^{r}],

where

[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}:=\left[\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 58.62308pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(1-\varphi/p^{r})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 105.24031pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 105.24031pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm
