153.57512pt\raise-16.64085pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{N}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 153.57512pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-48.33481pt\raise-33.28171pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.74107pt\raise-26.2867pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.24501pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(1-\varphi/p^{r-1})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 105.24031pt\raise-33.28171pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 105.24031pt\raise-33.28171pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}\right].

Proof.

The proof is given in
[
16
, Section 5.1]
. Let us recall how it goes to see that it still works in the condensed setting: We work locally. The quasi-isomorphism for the local statement is induced by the quasi-isomorphisms
\iota_{\rm BK}^{1}
and
\iota_{\rm BK}^{2}
given by the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({X})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{st}}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\rm BK}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\rm BK}^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({X})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({X})/F^{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\sim

\scriptstyle{\iota_{{\rm BK},r}}

where the quasi-isomorphism
\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{st}}
is the quasi-isomorphism from the proof of Proposition
3.18
and
\iota_{{\rm BK},r}
is the one from (
3.4
).
∎

We will prove below in Proposition
6.4
that we have an analogous result for the compactly supported cohomology. Let us first define and prove some properties satisfied by the twisted Hyodo-Kato cohomology and the quotients of
{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}
-cohomology. We take
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
.

(i)
Twisted Hyodo-Kato cohomology.

We set

\displaystyle\operatorname{HK}_{*}(X,r):=[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}.

Lemma 6.2
.

There is a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})
:

\operatorname{HK}_{c}(X,r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}[\operatorname{HK}(X,r)\to\operatorname{HK}(\partial X,r)]

where
\operatorname{HK}(\partial X,r):=\operatorname{colim}_{Z\in\Phi}\operatorname{HK}(X\setminus Z,r)
.

Proof.

We need to show that we have a quasi-isomorphism

[(\operatorname{colim}_{Z\in\Phi}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X\setminus Z))\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{colim}_{Z\in\Phi}([{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X\setminus Z)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}).

But this follows from the fact that taking the colimit commutes with
\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}
.
∎

(ii)
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology and its quotients.

Recall that we set

\displaystyle\operatorname{DR}_{*}(X,r):={\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},*}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}.

Arguing as in the proof of Lemma
6.2
we get:

Lemma 6.3
.

There is a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})
:

\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X,r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}[\operatorname{DR}(X,r)\to\operatorname{DR}(\partial X,r)]

where
\operatorname{DR}(\partial X,r):=\operatorname{colim}_{Z\in\Phi}\operatorname{DR}(X\setminus Z,r)
.

(iii)
Comparison with syntomic cohomology.
As in the classical case, we can compute compactly supported syntomic cohomology in terms of compactly supported Hyodo-Kato and
{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}
- cohomologies.

Proposition 6.4
.

There is a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})
:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\xrightarrow{\sim}[\operatorname{HK}_{c}(X,r)\to\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X,r)]

such that for all quasi-compact open
Z\subset X
, the following diagram commutes in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})
:

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{HK}_{c}(X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X,r)}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},Z}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{HK}_{Z}(X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DR}_{Z}(X,r).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Proof.

We deduce from Lemma
6.2
and Lemma
6.3
that we have a commutative diagram in which rows are distinguished triangles:

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(\partial X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{HK}_{c}(X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{HK}(X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{HK}(\partial X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DR}(X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DR}(\partial X,r)}

The second column is a distinguished triangle by Theorem
6.1
, so it suffices to check that the third column is a distinguished triangle as well. But for
Z\subset X
a quasi-compact open, we have:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X\setminus Z,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}[\operatorname{HK}(X\setminus Z,r)\to\operatorname{DR}(X\setminus Z,r)]

and we deduce the result by taking the colimit over all
Z\in\Phi
.
∎

6.2.
Overconvergent setting

The definition of the compactly supported syntomic cohomology of a dagger variety is the same as the one of the pro-étale cohomology: we first give a local definition using presentation of dagger affinoids, and then (co)sheafify it.

6.2.1.
Definition

If
X
is a dagger affinoid over
C
with a presentation
\{X_{h}\}_{h\in{\bf N}}
we define in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn}}(\partial X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

\displaystyle:={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(\partial\{X\}_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

\displaystyle:={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(\{X\}_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=[{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,r)\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn}}(\partial X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))],\quad\text{ for $r\in{\bf N}$}.

For a general smooth dagger variety
X
over
L
, using the natural equivalence

{\rm CSh}({\rm SmAff}^{\dagger}_{L,{\rm an}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm CSh}({\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{L,{\rm an}})\

we define the cosheaf
{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(r)
on
X_{\operatorname{an}}
as the cosheaf associated to the precosheaf defined by
U\mapsto{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(U,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),U\in{\rm SmAff}^{\dagger}_{L}
,
U\to X
an admissible open. We set

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):={\rm R}\Gamma(X,{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(r))\in{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}).

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
X
be in
{\rm Sm}_{C}^{\dagger}
.

(1)

(Local-global compatibility)
If
X
is a dagger affinoid then the natural map in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

is a quasi-isomorphism.

(2)

(Passing to completion)
If
X
is partially proper then there is natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}({X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(\widehat{X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

Proof.

For the first claim,
it suffices to show that for any open covering
X=X_{1}\cup X_{2}
by dagger affinoids, we have the Mayer-Vietoris distinguished triangle

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\leftarrow{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{1},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\oplus{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\leftarrow{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{1}\cap X_{2},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

Choosing a presentation
\{X_{h}\}
of
X
, an
h\in{\bf N}
, and using excision we can write this triangle as

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},\widehat{X}}(X_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\leftarrow{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},\widehat{X}_{1}}(X_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\oplus{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},\widehat{X}_{2}}(X_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\leftarrow{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}\widehat{X_{1}\cap X_{2}}}(X_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

That this triangle is distinguished can be seen by passing to twisted Hyodo-Kato and
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomologies.

Let us now prove claim (2).
If
X
is Stein, take
\{U_{n}\}
an increasing covering of
X
by admissible dagger affinoids. We have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(\widehat{X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(\widehat{X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{colim}_{n}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(\widehat{X}\setminus\widehat{U}_{n},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\sim

\textstyle{\operatorname{colim}_{n}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X\setminus{U}_{n},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\sim

where the second row is the distinguished triangle (
6.7
). (We note that there is no circular reasoning here: Remark
6.6
uses only the first claim of this lemma.) The second and third vertical arrows are quasi-isomorphisms by the usual comparison results between overconvergent cohomologies and their rigid analytic analogues (see
[
15
]
). We obtain that the first vertical map (defined by the diagram) is a quasi-isomorphism as well.

Since a general partially proper variety
X
is locally Stein we can conclude by analytic codescent.
∎

Remark 6.6
.

As for pro-étale cohomology, if
X
is smooth and Stein and
\{U_{n}\}
an increasing quasi-compact covering of
X
with
U_{n}\Subset U_{n+1}
, we define the cohomology of the boundary of
X
as

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(\partial X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=\operatorname{colim}_{U_{n}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X\setminus U_{n},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

The same proof as for (
4.6
) shows that we have a distinguished triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}})
:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(\partial X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

(6.7)

6.2.2.
Passage to the Bloch-Kato version

As in the rigid analytic, we can express compactly supported syntomic cohomology in terms of compactly supported de Rham and Hyodo-Kato cohomologies. We define the twisted Hyodo-Kato cohomology and the quotients of the
{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-cohomology as in the rigid case:

\displaystyle\operatorname{HK}_{c}(X,r):=[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}},

\displaystyle\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X,r):={\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}.

The first one lives in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})
, the second one in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})
.

Proposition 6.8
.

For all
i\geq 0
and
r\geq 0
, we have in
{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}

\displaystyle H^{i}\operatorname{HK}_{*}(X,r)\simeq(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},*}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}.

Proof.

The result for the compactly supported cohomology follows from the result in the classical case working with dagger affinoids and using the long exact sequence defining the cohomology with compact support. So it suffices to prove the result for
\operatorname{HK}(X,r)
. An analogous result in the classical setting was proved in
[
10
, Example 3.16]
(and extended in
[
16
, Prop. 4.24]
). The proof in the condensed setting is similar, the main difference is that
{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}}^{+}
is already flat for the solid tensor product
-\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}-
.

We first prove that we have

H^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})\simeq H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}},\quad\text{for all }i\geq 0.

(6.9)

Since
\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}
is flat over
F
, we have a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

H^{0}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}\simeq H^{0}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}.

This yields the distinguished triangle:

(H^{0}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})[0]\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}\to(\tau_{\leq 1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X))\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}

and, by taking the cohomology, we get

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}\simeq H^{0}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}),

\displaystyle H^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})\simeq H^{i}((\tau_{\leq 1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X))\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}),\quad\text{for all }i\geq 1.

We repeat the same computation to obtain the isomorphism (
6.9
) for all
i
.

We now assume that
X:={\mathscr{X}}_{C}
for
{\mathscr{X}}
in the category of basic semistable formal models
{\mathscr{M}}_{C}^{\dagger,\rm ss,b}
. The proposition in that case follows from the isomorphism (
6.9
) and from the two exact sequences:

\displaystyle 0\to M\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}\to M\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}}^{+}\xrightarrow{N}M\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}}^{+}\to 0,

\displaystyle 0\to(M\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+})^{\varphi=p^{r}}\to M\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}\xrightarrow{p^{r}-\varphi}M\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}\to 0

for
M
a finite
(\varphi,N)
-module (the first one is proved by recurrence on the integer
m
such that
N^{m}=0
and the second one is proved in the same way as in
[
14
, Remark 2.30]
).

In the general case, take
{\mathscr{U}}_{\bullet}
an étale hypercovering of
X
from
{\mathscr{M}}_{C}^{\dagger,\rm ss,b}
. By the previous paragraph, the proposition is true for each
{\mathscr{U}}_{i,C}
. We then use the two following spectral sequences to conclude:

\displaystyle E_{2}^{i,j}=H^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{U}}_{j,C})\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})\Rightarrow H^{i+j}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})

\displaystyle E_{2}^{i,j}=H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{U}}_{j,C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}\Rightarrow H^{i+j}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}

(to get the second one, we use the flatness of
\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}
and the fact that the differentials are
\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}
-linear).
∎

As in the rigid case, we can write syntomic cohomology in terms of Hyodo-Kato and de Rham cohomologies:

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
. There is a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})
:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\xrightarrow{\sim}[\operatorname{HK}_{c}(X,r)\to\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X,r)].

Proof.

It suffices to show the result for the
\natural
-cohomologies. Let
X
be an affinoid dagger space and
\{X_{h}\}
be a presentation of
X
. Consider the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn}}(\partial X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{HK}^{\natural}_{c}(X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{HK}^{\natural}(X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{HK}^{\natural}(\partial X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DR}^{\natural}_{c}(X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DR}^{\natural}(X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DR}^{\natural}(\partial X,r),}

where we set

\displaystyle\operatorname{HK}^{\natural}_{c}(X,r):=[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}},

\displaystyle\operatorname{DR}^{\natural}_{c}(X,r):={\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}.

The first row in the above diagram is a distinguished triangle by definition, the second and third ones are clearly distinguished triangles. The second and third column are distinguished triangles by Proposition
6.1
. It follows that the first column is a distinguished triangle as well.
∎

We will need the following:

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
j:X\hookrightarrow Y
be an open immersion of smooth dagger varieties over
C
, then the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(Y,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{HK}_{c}(X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{HK}_{c}(Y,r).}

Similarly, for the Hyodo-Kato map
\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},c}(-)\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(-)
.

Proof.

We can work locally. Let
X\hookrightarrow Y
be an open immersion of dagger affinoids and take
\{X_{h}\}
and
\{Y_{h}\}
presentations of
X
and
Y
respectively, such that we have open immersions
X_{h}\hookrightarrow Y_{h}
.
Recall that the maps
j_{*}:{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,r)\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(Y,r)
and
\operatorname{HK}_{c}(X,r)\to\operatorname{HK}_{c}(Y,r)
are induced by the compositions:

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,r)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},\widehat{X}}(X_{h},r)\xleftarrow{\sim}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},\widehat{X}}(Y_{h},r)\to\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},\widehat{Y}}(Y_{h},r)={\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(Y,r),

\displaystyle\operatorname{HK}^{\natural}_{c}(X,r)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{colim}_{h}\operatorname{HK}^{\natural}_{\widehat{X}}(X_{h},r)\xleftarrow{\sim}\operatorname{colim}_{h}\operatorname{HK}^{\natural}_{\widehat{X}}(Y_{h},r)\to\operatorname{colim}_{h}\operatorname{HK}^{\natural}_{\widehat{Y}}(Y_{h},r)\xleftarrow{\sim}\operatorname{HK}^{\natural}_{c}(Y,r).

The commutativity of the diagram in the lemma is now clear. The argument for the Hyodo-Kato map is analogous.
∎

6.3.
Period morphism

We show that we have a period isomorphism comparing syntomic cohomologies with pro-étale cohomologies with compact support from the previous section. This will allow us to compute explicitly
p
-adic pro-étale cohomologies in the next chapter of the paper.

6.3.1.
Rigid analytic period morphism

Recall that, for all
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
, we have a period morphism in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

\alpha_{r}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

that is a quasi-isomorphism after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
(see
[
15
, Corollary 7.3]
). By functoriality, it induces a period morphism in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

\alpha_{r,c}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

(6.12)

Theorem 6.13
.

For
X
in
\operatorname{Sm}_{C}
and
r\geq 0
, the map
\alpha_{r,c}
is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}({{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}})
:

\alpha_{r,c}:\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\xrightarrow{\sim}\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

Proof.

This follows from the map of distinguished triangles:

\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{\leq r}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(\partial X,r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{\leq r}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(\partial X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).}

The second and the third vertical maps are quasi-isomorphisms (after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
). Note here that truncation commutes with colimits. Hence the first vertical map is a quasi-isomorphism after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
as well.
∎

6.3.2.
Overconvergent period morphism

Let
X
be a dagger affinoid over
C
and let
\{X_{h}\}
be a presentation of
X
. We have compatible period maps in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),\quad\text{ and }\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

that are quasi-ismorphisms after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
. They induce a local period morphism for the compactly supported cohomology:

\alpha_{r,c}^{\natural}:{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\natural}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),

and this map is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p.\Box})
after taking the truncation.

We can know globalize the above construction and we obtain:

Theorem 6.14
.

For
X
in
\operatorname{Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
and
r\geq 0
, there is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})
:

\alpha^{\dagger}_{r,c}:\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\xrightarrow{\sim}\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

6.3.3.
Geometrization of the period morphism

We define
{\rm TVS}
(for Topological Vector Spaces) as the category of presheaves of solid
{\bf Q}_{p}
-modules (or, more generally, derived solid
{\bf Q}_{p}
-modules) on the category
{\rm Perf}_{C}
of perfectoid spaces over
C
. The usual Fontaine’s period rings

{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+},{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+},{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}}^{+},{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}},{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}},{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}}

have canonical structures of
{\rm TVS}
’s that we will denote by
{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+},{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+},{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{st}}^{+},{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{dR}},{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{cr}},{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{st}}
, respectively. As in
[
16
, §7]
, we will see that we can endow the cohomologies defined in the previous sections with a
{\rm TVS}
-structure and that the period morphism can be lifted to this category.

(i)
Rigid analytic setting.
If
X
is a smooth rigid analytic variety over
C
, we write
{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}),i\in{\bf N}
, (respectively
{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})
) for the functor on
{\rm Perf}_{C}
defined as

\Lambda\mapsto H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\Lambda},{\bf Q}_{p})\quad\text{(respectively, }\Lambda\mapsto{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\Lambda}{\bf Q}_{p})),

where
X_{\Lambda}
is the base change
X\times_{C}\Lambda
. These define objects in
{\rm TVS}
.

On the other hand, the syntomic cohomology can be geometrized by tensoring
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}}^{+}
with
-\otimes_{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}}^{+}}^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{st}}^{+}
and
F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+})
with
-\otimes_{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}}^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}
to obtain
{\mathbb{R}}_{\rm syn}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),r\geq 0
.

Recall that the period morphism can be lifted to
{\rm TVS}
:

Proposition 6.15
.

(
Colmez-Nizioł,
[
16
, Th. 7.3]
)

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
and
r\geq 0
. The functorial period map in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

\alpha_{r}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm syn}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

lifts to a functorial map of TVS’s (with values in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})
) :

{\bbalpha}_{r}:{\mathbb{R}}_{\rm syn}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),

which is a quasi-isomorphism after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
.

Similarly, for
X
a smooth rigid analytic variety over
C
, we endow
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))
and
{\rm R}\Gamma_{{\rm syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))
with a
{\rm TVS}
-structure by setting

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):\Lambda\mapsto{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\Lambda},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}_{{\rm syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):\Lambda\mapsto[[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}_{\breve{F}}{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{st}}^{+}(\Lambda)]^{\varphi=p^{r},N=0}\to({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+})\otimes_{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}}^{{\mathrm{L}}_{\Box}}{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(\Lambda))/F^{r}].

There are canonical quasi-isomorphisms:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

\displaystyle\simeq[{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(\partial X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)],

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}_{{\rm syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

\displaystyle\simeq[{\mathbb{R}}_{\rm syn}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\rm syn}(\partial X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)],

where
{\mathbb{R}}_{*}(\partial X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=\operatorname{colim}_{Z\in\Phi}{\mathbb{R}}_{*}(X\setminus Z,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))
, for
*\in\{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},{\rm syn}\}
. It follows easily from Proposition
6.15
that the period morphism for compactly supported cohomology can be lifted to the category
{\rm TVS}
:

Proposition 6.16
.

There exists a natural map of
{\rm TVS}
(with values in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})
) :

{\bbalpha}_{r,c}:{\mathbb{R}}_{{\rm syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),

(6.17)

lifting the morphism (
6.12
). Moreover, it is a quasi-isomorphism after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
.

(ii)
The overconvergent setting.
We have a similar picture for dagger varieties. Let
X
be a dagger affinoid over
C
, let
\{X_{h}\}_{h\in{\bf N}}
be a presentation, and
\widehat{X}
the associated rigid analytic variety. We define the
{\rm TVS}
:

{\mathbb{R}}_{*,c}^{\dagger}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=\operatorname{colim}_{h}[{\mathbb{R}}_{*}(X_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{R}}_{*}(X_{h}\setminus\widehat{X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))],\quad r\geq 0,\quad\text{ for }*\in\{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},{\rm syn}\}.

We then extend this definition to any smooth dagger variety
X
over
C
, by étale cosheafification and get
{\rm TVS}
’s:
{\mathbb{R}}_{*,c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))
.

Now, for smooth dagger affinoids
X
over
C
, using the rigid analytic period morphism
\bbalpha_{r,c}
from (
6.17
), we get a period morphism of
{\rm TVS}
’s

{\bbalpha}^{\dagger}_{r,c}:{\mathbb{R}}_{{\rm syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),\quad r\geq 0,

which globalizes to a period morphism
that lifts the period morphism
\alpha^{\dagger}_{r,c}
from Theorem
6.14
. It is a quasi-isomorphism after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
. To sum up:

Proposition 6.18
.

There exists a natural map of
{\rm TVS}
(with values in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})
) :

{\bbalpha}^{\dagger}_{r,c}:{\mathbb{R}}_{{\rm syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),

(6.19)

lifting the morphism (
6.14
). It is a quasi-isomorphism after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
.

7.
Computations

We present here computations of geometric compactly supported
p
-adic pro-étale cohomology of some Stein varieties using the period morphisms from Section
2
and the duality results from Section
5
. In particular, our computations show the existence of trace maps that will be used to prove Poincaré duality for geometric
p
-adic pro-étale cohomology in
[
13
]
.

7.1.
Affine space

Let us first consider the case of the affine rigid space of dimension
d
over
K
. Using the distinguished triangle from Proposition
6.4
, we can compute its compactly supported syntomic cohomology.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
X:={\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{d}
,
d\geq 1
, be the rigid analytic affine space of dimension
d
over
K
.

(1)

Let
r\geq 0
.
We have the following isomorphisms in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=0,\quad i<d,i>2d;

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\simeq(H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{i-d-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d)\otimes_{K}^{\Box}{\rm gr}_{F}^{r-i+d}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}},\quad d\leq i\leq 2d-1.

(2)

If
0\leq r<d
then
H_{\operatorname{syn},c}^{2d}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=0
. If
r\geq d
then we have an exact sequence in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}

0\to(H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{d-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d)(r-d)\to H_{\operatorname{syn},c}^{2d}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\bf Q}_{p}(r-d)\to 0.

Proof.

Clearly
H^{i}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=0
, for
i<d
.

Let
0\leq i\leq d-1
. Since

H^{i+d}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C},r)=H^{i+d-1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C},r)=0,

we obtain from Lemma
3.16
that

H^{i+d}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\simeq H^{i+d-1}\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X_{C},r)\simeq(H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{i-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d)\otimes^{\Box}_{K}{\rm gr}_{F}^{r-i}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}.

For the same reasons
H^{i+d}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=0
if
i>d+1
.

For
i=d
, by Section
5.5.1
, we have

H^{2d}\operatorname{HK}_{c}(X_{C},r)\simeq(\breve{F}\{-d\}\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{\breve{F}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}=(\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r-d}}=(\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{r-d}}.

And, by Lemma
3.16
, we have

H^{2d}\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X_{C},r)\simeq{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r-d}.

Hence we get the exact sequence:

0\to H^{2d-1}\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X_{C},r)\to H_{\operatorname{syn},c}^{2d}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to(\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{r-d}}\to{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r-d}\to H_{\operatorname{syn},c}^{2d+1}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to 0.

If
r<d
, this yields

H_{\operatorname{syn},c}^{2d}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=H_{\operatorname{syn},c}^{2d+1}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=0.

If
r\geq d
, we have a fundamental exact sequence of
p
-adic Hodge Theory:

0\to{\bf Q}_{p}(r-d)\to(\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{r-d}}\to{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r-d}\to 0.

Hence
H_{\operatorname{syn},c}^{2d+1}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=0
and we get the exact sequence

0\to(H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{d-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d)(r-d)\to H_{\operatorname{syn},c}^{2d}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\bf Q}_{p}(r-d)\to 0,

as wanted.
∎

The following corollary follows immediately from Proposition
7.1
and the period isomorphism from Theorem
6.13
.

Corollary 7.2
.

Let
X:={\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{d}
,
d\geq 1
, be the rigid analytic affine space of dimension
d
over
K
.

(1)

We have the following isomorphisms in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})=0,\quad i<d,i>2d;

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(i-d))\simeq H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{i-d-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d\mbox{ if }d\leq i\leq 2d-1.

(2)

We have an exact sequence in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}

0\to H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{d-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d\to H_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}^{2d}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))\to{\bf Q}_{p}\to 0.

Example 7.3

(Dimension 1)
.

For
d=1
and
r\geq 0
, Proposition
7.1
yields:

i)

H^{0}_{\operatorname{syn},c}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{1},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=0
,
H^{i}_{\operatorname{syn},c}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{1},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=0
, for
i\geq 3
.

ii)

H^{1}_{\operatorname{syn},c}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{1},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=0.

iii)

H^{2}_{\operatorname{syn},c}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{1},{\bf Q}_{p})=0
.

iv)

For
r\geq 1
, an exact sequence of solid
{\bf Q}_{p}
-modules :

0\to H^{1}_{c}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{1},{\mathscr{O}})(r-1)\to H^{2}_{\operatorname{syn},c}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{1},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\bf Q}_{p}(r-1)\to 0.

In the last point we have used the fact that the map
H^{1}_{c}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{1},{\mathscr{O}})\to H^{1}_{c}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{1},\Omega^{1})
is injective (since
H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR},c}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{1})=0
).

This yields:

(1)

H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{1},{\bf Q}_{p})=0
, for
i\neq 2
.

(2)

an exact sequence of solid
{\bf Q}_{p}
-modules

0\to H^{1}_{c}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{1},{\mathscr{O}})\to H^{2}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}({\mathbb{A}}_{C}^{1},{\bf Q}_{p}(1))\to{\bf Q}_{p}\to 0.

7.2.
Stein curves

We now compute the compactly supported
p
-adic cohomology of Stein curves. Let
X
be a smooth Stein variety over
K
of dimension
d=1
.
For
i\geq 0
, we have an exact sequence

\textstyle{H^{i-1}{\rm HK}_{c}(X_{C},i)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{i-1}{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},i)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(i))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{i}{\rm HK}_{c}(X_{C},i)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{i}{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},i)}

We have in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})
:

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}

\displaystyle\simeq(H^{1}_{c}(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to H^{1}_{c}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})[-1],

\displaystyle F^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\mathrm{L}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle\simeq(H^{1}_{c}(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}F^{i}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to H^{1}_{c}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}F^{i-1}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})[-1],

\displaystyle{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},i)

\displaystyle\simeq(H^{1}_{c}(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{i})\to H^{1}_{c}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{i-1}))[-1].

In particular
H^{j}{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},i)=0
, for
j\neq 1,2.

We also have (see Proposition
6.8
):

H^{j}{\rm HK}_{c}(X_{C},i)\simeq(H^{j}_{{\rm HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{i}}.

(7.4)

By the Poincaré duality

H^{j}_{{\rm HK},c}(X_{C})\simeq H^{2-j}_{{\rm HK}}(X_{C})^{*}\{-1\},\quad H^{j}_{{\rm dR},c}(X_{C})\simeq H^{2-j}_{{\rm dR}}(X_{C})^{*}\{-1\},

where the first dual is the
F^{\rm nr}
-dual and the second one – the
C
-dual. It follows that

\displaystyle H^{2}_{{\rm HK},c}(X_{C})\simeq F^{\rm nr}\{-1\};\quad H^{i}_{{\rm HK},c}(X_{C})=0,\mbox{ for }i\neq 1,2;

(7.5)

\displaystyle H^{2}_{{\rm dR},c}(X_{C})\simeq C\{-1\};\quad H^{i}_{{\rm dR},c}(X_{C})=0,\mbox{ for }i\neq 1,2.

This implies that
H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})=0
for
i\neq 1,2,3
.

\bullet

The group
H^{1}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})
.

We have the exact sequence of solid
{\bf Q}_{p}
-modules

\textstyle{H^{0}{\rm HK}_{c}(X_{C},2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}{\rm HK}_{c}(X_{C},1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},1)}

By (
7.5
), this yields the exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}{\rm HK}_{c}(X_{C},1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},1).}

(7.6)

From Lemma
3.14
, we obtain an isomorphism

H^{1}\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X_{C},1)\simeq H^{1}_{c}(X_{C},{\mathscr{O}})

and from Proposition
6.8
,
H^{1}\operatorname{HK}_{c}(X_{C},1)\simeq(H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p}
. But the map

(H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p}\to H^{1}_{c}(X_{C},{\mathscr{O}})

factorizes as

(H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p}\to H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C})\to H^{1}_{c}(X_{C},{\mathscr{O}})

(7.7)

where the second morphism is injective. So the kernel of the second arrow in (
7.6
) is isomorphic to the kernel of the first arrow in (
7.7
). This is equal to
(H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=1}
(see
[
10
, Lemma 3.39]
or Lemma
8.1
below) and so we get

H^{1}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(1))\xrightarrow{\sim}(H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=1}.

\bullet

The group
H^{2}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})
.
The long exact sequence above gives the exact sequence:

\textstyle{H^{1}{\rm HK}_{c}(X_{C},2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}{\rm HK}_{c}(X_{C},2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},2)}

Using (
7.4
) and (
7.5
) we get the exact sequence

\textstyle{H^{1}{\rm HK}_{c}(X_{C},2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+,\varphi=p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{1}}

(7.8)

Recall now that we have a fundamental exact sequence

0\to{\bf Q}_{p}(1)\to{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+,\varphi=p}\to{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{1}\to 0.

Using it we get
the exact sequence

\textstyle{H^{1}{\rm HK}_{c}(X_{C},2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf Q}_{p}(1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

(7.9)

where the first term is isomorphic to
(H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{2},N=0}
.

Remark 7.10
.

By duality, the slopes of the Frobenius on
H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})
are between
0
and
1
. If we assume they are
0
, then, we can show that we have an exact sequence

0\to(H^{1}_{c}(X_{C},{\mathscr{O}})/\operatorname{Ker}d)(1)\to H^{2}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2))\to{\bf Q}_{p}(1)\to 0.

(7.11)

To see that first note that, from Lemma
3.14
, we get an exact sequence

0\to H^{1}_{c}(X_{C},{\mathscr{O}})(1)\to H^{1}{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},2)\to H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C})\to 0

(7.12)

and since the slopes of the Frobenius on
H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})
are
\leq 1
, we have the following exact sequence (see
[
10
, Lemma 3.39]
or Lemma
8.1
below)

0\to(H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p,N=0}\xrightarrow{t}(H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{2},N=0}\to H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C})\to 0.

(7.13)

Combining (
7.12
) and (
7.13
), we see that the cokernel of the first map in (
7.9
) is isomorphic to the cokernel of the map

(H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}t\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{2},N=0}\to H^{1}_{c}(X_{C},{\mathscr{O}})(1)

which in turn factorizes as

(H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}t\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{2},N=0}\to H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C})(1)\to H^{1}_{c}(X_{C},{\mathscr{O}})(1).

But, if the slopes of the Frobenius are zero then the first map is surjective (see Lemma
8.1
below) so the cokernel we want is isomorphic to the cokernel of the map on the right, which gives the exact sequence (
7.11
).

\bullet

The group
H^{3}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})
.

We claim that
H^{3}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})=0
. Indeed, we have the exact sequence

\textstyle{H^{2}{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},3)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{3}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(3))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{3}{\rm HK}_{c}(X_{C},3)}

But the two terms, on the left and on the right, are zero (use Lemma
3.14
for the left term).

7.3.
Trace maps

The purpose of this section is to define trace maps for the
p
-adic pro-étale cohomology of smooth partially proper varieties and dagger affinoids of dimension
d
over
K
. To do that, we use the period isomorphism to express
H^{2d}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))
in terms of Hyodo-Kato and de Rham cohomologies and then we use the trace morphisms from Section
5
. We then show that these trace maps satisfy various compatibilities.

7.3.1.
Partially proper varieties and dagger affinoids

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper variety or a smooth dagger affinoid over
K
. Assume that it is of dimension
d\geq 1
and geometrically irreducible.
We have an exact sequence of solid
{\bf Q}_{p}
-modules

\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(H^{2d}_{\rm HK,c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{2d}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2d}{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},2d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(7.14)

Since
N=0
and
\varphi=p^{d}
on
H^{2d}_{\rm HK,c}(X_{C})
, we get an exact sequence

\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\rm HK,c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+,\varphi=p^{d}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\rm dR,c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{d}}

Via the compatible trace maps

{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{HK},X_{C}}:H^{2d}_{\rm HK,c}(X_{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}F^{\operatorname{nr}},\quad{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR},X}:H^{2d}_{\rm dR,c}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}K

this yields an exact sequence

\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+,\varphi=p^{d}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Hence a natural trace map of solid
{\bf Q}_{p}
-modules

{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},X_{C}}:H^{2d}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))\to{\bf Q}_{p}(d).

This map is surjective. We will denote in the same way the derived trace map in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},X_{C}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))[2d]\to{\bf Q}_{p}(d)

obtained as the composition

\displaystyle{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},X_{C}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))[2d]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\tau_{\leq 2d}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))[2d]

\displaystyle\to H^{2d}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},X_{C}}}}{\bf Q}_{p}(d).

Remark 7.15
.

(Proper varieties) Assume that
X
is proper in the above.
Then we see that the first map in the exact sequence (
7.14
) is injective and it follows that the trace map is an isomorphism

{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},X_{C}}:H^{2d}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\bf Q}_{p}(d).

7.3.2.
Properties of trace maps

The trace morphisms defined above satisfy the following compatibilities.

Lemma 7.16
.

The trace map
{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},X_{C}}
is compatible with

(1)

the corresponding Hyodo-Kato and de Rham trace maps, i.e., the following diagrams commute in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}

\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},X_{C}}}
\textstyle{(H^{2d}_{{\rm HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}}^{+})^{N=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{HK},X_{C}}\otimes{\rm Id}}
\textstyle{{\bf Q}_{p}(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}\{-d\},}

\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},X_{C}}}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{{\rm dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{\operatorname{dR},X}\otimes{\rm Id}}
\textstyle{{\bf Q}_{p}(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}

(2)

open immersions
j:X\hookrightarrow Y
, where
X,Y
are or partially proper varieties or dagger affinoids over
K
.

Proof.

The compatibility (1) is clear from the construction. For compatibility (2), we first assume that
X
and
Y
are partially proper and consider the following diagram of solid
{\bf Q}_{p}
-modules:

\textstyle{(H^{2d}_{{\rm HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}}^{+})^{N=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{X_{C}}\otimes{\rm Id}}
\textstyle{(H^{2d}_{{\rm HK},c}(Y_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}}^{+})^{N=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{Y_{C}}\otimes{\rm Id}}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{X_{C}}}
\textstyle{H^{2d}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(Y_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm tr}_{Y_{C}}}
\textstyle{{\bf Q}_{p}(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf Q}_{p}(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}\{-d\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}\{-d\}}

We want to show that the upper square is commutative. We know that the upper trapezoid is commutative. The right and left triangles are commutative by claim (1). Since the arrows from
{\bf Q}_{p}(d)
to
{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+,\varphi=p^{d}}
are injective, it suffices then to prove the commutativity of the outer trapezoid. or that the geometric Hyodo-Kato trace is compatible with the open immersion
X\hookrightarrow Y
. But this was shown in Lemma
5.33
.

The proof is similar when
X
and
Y
are both dagger affinoids, using the proof of Theorem
5.34
.
∎

7.3.3.
Comparison with the Huber trace map

This section will not be used in the rest of the paper. Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid analytic variety of dimension
d
, over
K
. Recall (see Lemma
2.3
) that we have a canonical map in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}),

where
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})=(\lim_{n}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Z}/p^{n}))\otimes^{\Box}_{{\bf Z}_{p}}{\bf Q}_{p}
. We also have (compatible with
n
) Huber trace maps (see
[
22
, Th. 7.5.3]
)

{\rm tr}^{H}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},X_{C},n}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Z}/p^{n}(d))[2d]\to{\bf Z}/p^{n}

that induce a trace map in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

{\rm tr}^{H}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},X_{C}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))[2d]\to{\bf Q}_{p}.

We will call the composition

{\rm tr}^{H}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},X_{C}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))[2d]\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))[2d]\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm tr}^{H}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},X_{C}}}}{\bf Q}_{p}

the
pro-étale Huber trace map
.

Proposition 7.17
.

The two trace maps in
{\mathscr{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

\operatorname{tr}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},X_{C}},\operatorname{tr}^{H}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},X_{C}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))[2d]\to{\bf Q}_{p}(d)

are equal.

Proof.

By the spectral sequence (
2.32
), we have a surjection in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}

\bigoplus_{i\in I}H^{2d}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(U_{i,C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))\twoheadrightarrow H^{2d}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d)),

where the index set
\{U_{i}\}
runs over a covering family of open discs. Hence we may assume that
X
is an open disc. Then, since both trace maps are compatible with
open immersions of partially proper rigid analytic varieties (by Lemma
7.16
and
[
40
, Th. 5.3.3]
, respectively), we may assume that
X
is the projective space:
X={\mathbb{P}}^{d,{\rm an}}_{C}
.

Now we can drop the compact support everywhere and then pass to the algebraic category. It suffices thus to compare the trace maps

\tilde{\operatorname{tr}}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},X_{C}},\operatorname{tr}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},X_{C}}:H^{2d}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d,{\rm alg}}_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2d))\to{\bf Q}_{p}(d),

(7.18)

where the first trace map is the algebraic analog of
\operatorname{tr}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},X_{C}}
(so defined via comparison theorems) and the second trace map is the classical algebraic étale trace map. That this passage works this way follows from GAGA-type theorems in
[
34
]
and the compatibility of the algebraic and analytic étale trace maps. Now, the equality of the two trace maps in (
7.18
) follows from the known fact that
the classical comparison theorems are compatible with trace maps (this reduces to a comparison of Chern class maps).
∎

8.
Stein varieties

We construct here a conditional version of a fundamental diagram for geometric compactly supported
p
-adic pro-étale cohomology of Stein varieties and apply it to determine such cohomology of tori and Drinfeld’s spaces. The proofs are similar to the ones of
[
10
]
, but more complicated due to the shift by
d
that appears in the term coming from the compactly supported de Rham cohomology.

8.1.
Fundamental diagram

Let
X
be a smooth dagger Stein variety of dimension
d
over
K
. The purpose of this section is to study the compactly supported
p
-adic pro-étale cohomology of
X_{C}
. To do that, we need to extend some of the results of
[
10
]
and, in particular, their Lemma 3.39:

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
j
,
k
and
l
be in
{\bf N}
such that
k\leq j
. There is an exact sequence in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}
:

0\to(H^{l}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{j-k}}\xrightarrow{t^{k+1}}(H^{l}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{j+1}}\xrightarrow{\pi^{l,j+1}_{k+1}}H^{l}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{k+1}).

Moreover, the right arrow is surjective if the slopes of the Frobenius on
H^{l}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})
are
\leq j-k
.

Proof.

The case
k=0
was proved in
[
10
, Lemma 3.39]
. For
k\neq 0
, it is clear that the multiplication by
t^{k+1}
is injective, it remains to show the exactness in the middle. To do that, as in
[
10
, Lemma 3.39]
, it suffices to show that for all
\delta
,
(F^{k+1}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{j+1-\delta}}=(t^{k+1}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{j+1-\delta}}
, where we set
F^{k}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}}=\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}}\cap F^{k}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}
. Clearly,
(t^{k+1}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{j+1-\delta}}\subset(F^{k+1}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{j+1-\delta}}.

We prove the other inclusion by induction. For
k=0
, we have

(F^{1}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{j+1-\delta}}\subset\{x\in\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}}\;|\;\varphi^{n}(x)\in F^{1}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}}\text{ for all }n>0\}=t\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}}.

Let
k\geq 0
. By induction, it suffices to prove that for all
\delta
,
(F^{k+1}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{j+1-\delta}}\subset(tF^{k}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{j+1-\delta}}
. Let
y
be in
(F^{k+1}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{j+1-\delta}}
, then
y=t^{k+1}y_{0}
with
y_{0}
in
{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}
and we have

y\in\{x\in\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}}\;|\;\varphi^{n}(x)\in F^{1}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}}\text{ for all }n>0\}=t\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}}.

We obtain that
t^{k}y_{0}
is in
\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}}\cap F^{k}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}=F^{k}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}}
, which concludes the proof of the first statement of the lemma.

Let us prove that if the slopes of the Frobenius on
H^{l}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})
are
\leq j-k
, then
\pi^{l,j+1}_{k+1}
is surjective. We show by induction that for all
1\leq m\leq k+1
, the map

\pi^{l,j+1}_{m}:(H^{l}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{j+1}}\to H^{l}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{m}

is surjective. For
m=1
, the result follows from
[
10
, Lemma 3.39]
(the slopes of the Frobenius are less or equal to
j-k
so they are less or equal to
j
). Assume that
\pi^{l,j+1}_{m-1}
is surjective. The surjectivity of
\pi^{l,j+1}_{m}
will follow from the commutative diagram:

\textstyle{H^{l}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}(F^{m-1}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{l}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{l}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{m-1})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces(H^{l}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}t^{m-1}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{j+1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(H^{l}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{j+1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{l,j+1}_{m}}
\textstyle{(H^{l}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{j+1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{l,j+1}_{m-1}}

The first row is exact, the vertical arrow
\pi^{l,j+1}_{m-1}
is surjective by the inductive assumption, and the left vertical arrow is surjective since the morphism

(H^{l}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{j+1-(m-1)}}\to H^{l}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{1})

is surjective (the slopes of the Frobenius are less or equal to
j-k
so as long as
m
is less than
k+1
, the slopes are less or equal to
j-m+1
). We deduce that
\pi^{l,j+1}_{m}
is surjective, as wanted. ∎

Remark 8.2
.

Since the slopes of Frobenius on
H^{l}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})
are between
0
and
i
, by duality, the slopes of the Frobenius on
H^{l}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})
are between
d-(2d-l)=l-d
and
d-0=d
.

Proposition 8.3
.

Assume that the maps
\pi^{r-1,r}_{d}
and
\pi^{r-1,r-d}_{1}
are surjective. Then there is an exact sequence in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}
:

0\to(H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d)(d)\to H_{\operatorname{syn},c}^{r}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}t^{d}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{r},N=0}\to 0.

Proof.

Consider the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(H^{d}_{c}(\Omega^{r-d})/{\rm Im}(d))(d-1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r}\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X_{C},r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}/F^{d-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\operatorname{HK}^{r}_{c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}t^{d-1}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{r}}
\textstyle{(\operatorname{HK}^{r}_{c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}/F^{d-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(H^{d}_{c}(\Omega^{r-d-1})/{\rm Im}(d))(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r-1}\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X_{C},r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r-1}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}/F^{d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\operatorname{HK}^{r-1}_{c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}t^{d}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{r-1}}
\textstyle{(\operatorname{HK}^{r-1}_{c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{r-1,r}_{d}}
\textstyle{H^{r-1}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}({\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}/F^{d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

where
\operatorname{HK}^{*}_{c}(X_{C}):=H^{*}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})
,
H^{d}_{c}(\Omega^{*}):=H^{d}_{c}(X_{C},\Omega^{*})
and the maps
\gamma_{r-1}
,
\gamma_{r}
are obtained by factorization. The rows are exact sequences by Lemma
3.14
and Lemma
8.1
(and by assumption on
\pi_{d}^{r-1,r}
for the bottow row). The column in the middle is an exact sequence as well.
We deduce that we have an exact sequence:

0\to\mathrm{coker}(\gamma_{r-1})\to H^{r}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to\operatorname{Ker}(\gamma_{r})\to 0

and it remains to compute
\operatorname{Ker}(\gamma_{r})
and
\operatorname{coker}(\gamma_{r-1})
.

(
\bullet
)
Kernel of
\gamma_{r}
.
The map
\gamma_{r}
factorizes as

(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}t^{d-1}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{r}^{(1)}}H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C})(d-1)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{r}^{(2)}}(H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d})/{\rm Im}(d))(d-1)

with
\gamma_{r}^{(2)}
injective. Then,

\operatorname{Ker}(\gamma_{r})\simeq\operatorname{Ker}(\gamma_{r}^{(1)})\simeq(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}t^{d}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}},

as wanted.

(
\bullet
)
Cokernel of
\gamma_{r-1}
.
As above, the map
\gamma_{r-1}
factorizes as

(H^{r-1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}t^{d}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{r-1}^{(1)}}H^{r-1}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C})(d)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{r-1}^{(2)}}(H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d-1})/{\rm Im}(d))(d)

with
\gamma_{r-1}^{(2)}
injective. The hypothesis that
\pi_{1}^{r-1,r-d}
is surjective implies that
\gamma_{r-1}^{(1)}
is surjective as well. So

\operatorname{coker}(\gamma_{r-1})\simeq\operatorname{coker}(\gamma_{r-1}^{(2)})\simeq(H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d)(d)

and we obtain the exact sequence from the proposition.
∎

Using the previous lemma, we obtain a bicartesian diagram analogous to the one of
[
10
, Example 3.35]
to compute the compactly supported syntomic cohomology of Stein varieties:

Theorem 8.4
.

Assume that the maps
\pi^{r-1,r}_{d}
and
\pi^{r-1,r-d}_{1}
are surjective. Then, there is a map of exact sequences in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}
:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d)(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{\operatorname{syn},c}^{r}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}t^{d}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{r},N=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d)(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d})^{d=0}(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C})(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

Proof.

The first row is exact by the previous proposition and the exactness of the second one is implied by the definition of the de Rham cohomology
H^{i+d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C})=H^{i}(H^{d}_{c}(X_{C},\Omega^{\bullet}))
.
The map
\alpha
is given by the composition:

H^{r}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to H^{r}(F^{r}(R\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))\to H_{c}^{d}(X,\Omega_{X}^{r-d})^{d=0}\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}(F^{d}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{d+1})

and
\beta
is the morphism:

(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}t^{d}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{r},N=0}\xrightarrow{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}F^{d}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to(H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{K}C)(d).

We then show that the diagram is commutative using the same argument as in the classical case
[
10
, Example 3.35]
.
∎

Corollary 8.5
.

Let
X
be a smooth Stein variety of dimension
d
over K and
r\geq 0
. Assume that the maps
\pi^{r-1,r}_{d}
and
\pi^{r-1,r-d}_{1}
are surjective.Then there is a bicartesian diagram in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}
:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern
