114.61118pt\raise-33.1081pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C})(d)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Remark 8.6
.

If the slopes of the Frobenius on
H^{r-1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})
are in
\{r-d-1,r-d\}
then the map
\pi^{r-1,r}_{d}
is surjective. If they are equal to
r-d-1
then
\pi^{r-1,r-d}_{1}
is surjective.

Remark 8.7
.

We will now show that, if we drop the surjectivity assumption for
\pi^{r-1,r}_{d}
(but we are still assuming that
\pi^{r-1,r-d}_{1}
is surjective), then there is a short exact sequence:

0\to\operatorname{coker}(\pi^{r-1,r}_{d})\to H_{\operatorname{syn},c}^{r}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to P(X_{C},r)\to 0

(8.8)

where
P(X_{C},r)
is the bicartesian diagram from the previous theorem, i.e.,

P(X_{C},r):=\operatorname{Ker}((H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}t^{d}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{r},N=0}\oplus H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d})^{d=0}(d)\to H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C})(d)).

We keep the same notations as in the proof of Propositon
8.3
and we moreover denote by

\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}^{r-1,r}
the map
(H^{r-1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{r},N=0}\to H^{r-1}\operatorname{DR}_{c}(X_{C},r)
. Then, similar computations as before show that we have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{\operatorname{coker}(\pi^{r-1,r}_{d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{coker}(\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}^{r-1,r})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{\operatorname{syn},c}^{r}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}t^{d}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{r},N=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d)(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d})^{d=0}(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C})(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where the rows and the first column are exact (the vertical exact sequence follows from the snake lemma and the computation of
\gamma_{r-1}
made in the proof of Proposition
8.3
:

\operatorname{coker}(\gamma_{r-1})\simeq(H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d)(d)

using the assumption that
\pi^{r-1,r-d}_{1}
is surjective).
The dotted arrow is obtained by factorization of
\alpha
and defines a section to the injection.

Now, the exact sequence (
8.8
) follows from a diagram chase (note that the map on the left in the exact sequence is given by a section of the morphism from the cokernel of
\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}^{r-1,r}
to the cokernel of
\pi^{r-1,r}_{d}
).

8.2.
Examples

We will now use Theorem
8.4
to compute the compactly supported geometric
p
-adic pro-étale cohomology of tori and Drinfeld spaces.

8.2.1.
Torus

We will use the previous formula to compute the compactly supported pro-étale cohomology of the torus
X:={\mathscr{G}}_{m,K}^{d}
. For
0\leq r\leq d-1
, we have
H^{r}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})=0
since
H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C})=0,
for
i\leq d-1
.
Assume thus that
d\leq r\leq 2d
. We start with the following

Lemma 8.9
.

For all
k\geq 0
, there is an isomorphism of solid
{\bf Q}_{p}
-modules

(H^{r-1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{k},N=0}\simeq(\wedge^{2d-r+1}{\bf Q}_{p}^{d})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}(\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{k+d-r+1}}.

Proof.

By Section
5.5.2
, we have:

(H^{r-1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{k},N=0}\simeq(\wedge^{2d-r+1}(F^{\rm nr})^{d}\{d-r+1\}\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{k},N=0}.

The monodromy on
\wedge^{2d-r+1}(F^{\rm nr})^{d}
is trivial.
We compute

\displaystyle(\wedge^{2d-r+1}(F^{\rm nr})^{d}\{d-r+1\}\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{k},N=0}

\displaystyle\simeq(\wedge^{2d-r+1}(F^{\rm nr})^{d}\{d-r+1\})^{\varphi=p^{-(d-r+1)}}\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}(\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{k+d-r+1}}

\displaystyle\simeq(\wedge^{2d-r+1}{\bf Q}_{p}^{d})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}(\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{k+d-r+1}},

which proves the lemma.
∎

Since we also have

H^{r-1}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\simeq(H_{\operatorname{dR}}^{2d-r+1}(X))^{*}\simeq\wedge^{2d-r+1}K^{d},

we deduce that the maps

\displaystyle\pi_{1}^{r-1,r-d}:

\displaystyle(\wedge^{2d-r+1}{\bf Q}_{p}^{d})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}(\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p}\to(\wedge^{2d-r+1}{\bf Q}_{p}^{d})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}C

\displaystyle\pi_{d}^{r-1,r}

\displaystyle(\wedge^{2d-r+1}{\bf Q}_{p}^{d})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}(\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{d+1}}\to(\wedge^{2d-r+1}{\bf Q}_{p}^{d})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}/F^{d}

are surjective.
The same computation as in the proof of the lemma gives:

\displaystyle(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}t^{d}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{r},N=0}\simeq(\wedge^{2d-r}{\bf Q}^{d}_{p})(d).

So, by Theorem
8.4
we obtain a map of exact sequences in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}
:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{d}_{c}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}^{r}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r-d))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\wedge^{2d-r}{\bf Q}_{p}^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{d}_{c}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d})^{d=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\wedge^{2d-r}C^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

(8.10)

For comparison, we have
H_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},c}^{r}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r-d))\simeq H_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},c}^{r}(X^{\rm alg}_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r-d))\simeq\wedge^{2d-r}{\bf Q}_{p}^{d}
.

Remark 8.11
.

Recall that for the usual cohomology we have an exact sequence in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}
(see
[
10
, Section 4.3.2]
),

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega^{r-1}(X_{C})/\operatorname{Ker}d\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}^{r}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\wedge^{r}{\bf Q}_{p}^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

We claim that, for any smooth Stein variety
X
over
C
, we have an isomorphism in
C_{\Box}

(\Omega^{r-1}(X)/\operatorname{Ker}d)^{*}\simeq H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d-r})/\operatorname{Ker}d.

(8.12)

This observation together with diagram (
8.10
) was a starting point of the study of dualities for
p
-adic pro-étale cohomology in
[
13
]
. To prove the isomorphism
(
8.12
), let
r^{\prime}=d-r
and consider the exact sequence

0\to\Omega^{r-1}(X)/\operatorname{Ker}d\to\Omega^{r}(X)^{d=0}\to H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\to 0.

Dualizing, we get the exact sequence (recall that
\operatorname{Ext}^{j}_{C_{\Box}}(H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X),C)
is zero for
j>0
since
H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)
is of compact type)

0\to H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)^{*}\to\Omega^{r}(X)^{d=0,*}\to(\Omega^{r-1}(X)/\operatorname{Ker}d)^{*}\to 0.

Using Poincaré duality from Theorem
5.23
, we obtain the exact sequence (where
r^{\prime}=d-r
)

0\to H^{d+r^{\prime}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{r^{\prime}})/{\rm Im}\,d\to(\Omega^{r-1}(X)/\operatorname{Ker}d)^{*}\to 0.

Hence an isomorphism (
8.12
), as wanted.

8.2.2.
Drinfeld’s space

We now treat the case of the Drinfeld space
X:=\mathbb{H}_{K}^{d}
. Recall that we have natural isomorphisms of solid
K
- and
F^{\rm nr}(\varphi,N)
-modules, respectively (see
[
10
, Lemma 5.11]
):

H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\mathbb{H}_{K}^{d})\simeq\operatorname{Sp}_{i}(K)^{*},\quad H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(\mathbb{H}_{C}^{d})\simeq\operatorname{Sp}_{i}(F^{\rm nr})^{*}\{-i\},\quad 0\leq i\leq d,

where
\operatorname{Sp}_{i}(-)
is the generalized locally constant Steinberg representation (see
[
10
, Sec. 5.2.1]
).
We note that the monodromy on
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(\mathbb{H}_{C}^{d})
is trivial. For
i>d
,
H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\mathbb{H}_{K}^{d})=0
. Hence we have natural isomorphisms of solid
K
- and
F^{\rm nr}(\varphi,N)
-modules, respectively:

H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(\mathbb{H}_{K}^{d})\simeq\operatorname{Sp}_{2d-i}(K),\quad H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(\mathbb{H}_{C}^{d})\simeq\operatorname{Sp}_{2d-i}(F^{\rm nr})\{d-i\},\quad i\geq d.

In particular, the monodromy on
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(\mathbb{H}_{C}^{d})
is trivial. For
i<d
, we have
H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(\mathbb{H}_{K}^{d})=0
, hence
H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(\mathbb{H}_{K}^{d},{\bf Q}_{p})=0
.

Assume thus that
r\geq d
.

Lemma 8.13
.

For all
k\geq 0
, we have a natural isomorphism of solid
{\bf Q}_{p}
-modules

(H^{r-1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{k},N=0}\simeq\operatorname{Sp}_{2d-r+1}({\bf Q}_{p})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}(\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{k+d-r+1}}.

Proof.

From the above formulas we get

\displaystyle(H^{r-1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{k},N=0}

\displaystyle\simeq(\operatorname{Sp}_{2d-r+1}(F^{\rm nr})\{d-r+1\}\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{k}}

\displaystyle\simeq(\operatorname{Sp}_{2d-r+1}(F^{\rm nr})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{k+d-r+1}}

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Sp}_{2d-r+1}({\bf Q}_{p})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}(\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{k+d-r+1}}.

∎

And as in the case of torus, we obtain that the maps

\displaystyle\pi_{1}^{r-1,r-d}:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Sp}_{2d-r+1}({\bf Q}_{p})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}(\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p}\to\operatorname{Sp}_{2d-r+1}({\bf Q}_{p})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}C,

\displaystyle\pi_{d}^{r-1,r}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Sp}_{2d-r+1}({\bf Q}_{p})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}(\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\rm cr}})^{\varphi=p^{d+1}}\to\operatorname{Sp}_{2d-r+1}({\bf Q}_{p})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}({\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}/F^{d})

are surjective. Since, computing as in the the proof of the above lemma, we also have

(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{F^{\rm nr}}t^{d}\operatorname{{\mathbf{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})^{\varphi=p^{r},N=0}\simeq\operatorname{Sp}_{2d-r}({\bf Q}_{p})(d),

from Theorem
8.4
, we obtain the map of exact sequences
in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}
:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{d}_{c}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}^{r}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r-d))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Sp}_{2d-r}({\bf Q}_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{d}_{c}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d-1})/\operatorname{Ker}d\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{c}^{d}(X_{C},\Omega^{r-d})^{d=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Sp}_{2d-r}(C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

(8.14)

For comparison, we have an isomorphism (see
[
11
, Th. 1.2]
)

H_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},c}^{r}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r-d))\simeq\operatorname{Sp}^{\rm cont}_{2d-r}({\bf Q}_{p}),

where
\operatorname{Sp}^{\rm cont}_{i}(-)
is the generalized continuous Steinberg representations (see
[
10
, Sec. 5.2.1]
). Hence we should have a commutative diagram (we did not check compatibilities)

\textstyle{H_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}^{r}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r-d))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Sp}_{2d-r}({\bf Q}_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t},c}^{r}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r-d))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Sp}^{\rm cont}_{2d-r}({\bf Q}_{p}).}
